BURKINA FASO IVE REPUBLIC

BURKINA FASO	IVE REPUBLIC-----------	SEVENTH LEGISLATURE
UNITY-PROGRESS-JUSTICE----------- NATIONAL ASSEMBLY

LAW NO. 025-2018/AN
ON THE PENAL CODE

THE NATIONAL ASSEMBLY

Having regard to	the Constitution;

Having regard to	resolution no. 001-2015/AN of 30 December 2015 on the validation of the mandate of deputies;

deliberated in its session of 31 May 2018 and adopted the law whose content follows:

BOOK I

GENERAL PROVISIONS TITLE I: CRIMINAL LAW

CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PRINCIPLES

Article 111-1

No offence may be punished and no penalty imposed unless they are legally provided for.

Article 111-2

Criminal law is to be strictly construed.
Only acts constituting an offence at the date on which they were committed are punishable.

Article 111-3

No one may be declared criminally liable and incur a sanction as a result unless he or she has committed an offence.

Article 111-4

There is no crime or offence without intent to commit it.
However, where the law so provides, there is an offence in the case of deliberate endangerment of the person of another and in the case of recklessness, negligence or breach of an obligation of care or safety provided for by law or regulation.
In the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph, natural persons who have not directly caused the damage, but who have created or contributed to creating the situation which enabled the damage to occur or who have not taken measures to avoid it, are criminally liable if it is established that they have either deliberately violated a particular obligation of care or safety provided for by law or regulation, or committed a gross fault which exposed others to a serious risk of which they could not have been unaware.

Article 111-5

Every person is entitled, in full equality, to a fair and public hearing by an independent and impartial tribunal, which shall determine either his or her rights and obligations or the merits of any criminal charge against him or her.

Article 111-6

Every person accused of committing an offence is presumed innocent until his or her guilt has been legally established.
No one may be found guilty of an offence or sentenced to a penalty otherwise than by decision of a competent court.

Article 111-7

Treaties, agreements or conventions duly ratified and published take precedence over domestic criminal provisions.

Article 111-8

There is a concurrence of offences where an offence is committed by a person before that person has been finally convicted of another offence.
In the event of conviction for several crimes or offences, only the most severe penalty shall be imposed.
Penalties are cumulative in the case of actual concurrence between contraventions, between offences and non-connected contraventions or between crimes and non-connected contraventions.

Article 111-9

Where an individual is the subject of several convictions for crimes or offences resulting from separate prosecutions before any court, merger of penalties must be ordered.
Where a principal penalty is the subject of a pardon, account must be taken, for the purposes of merger of penalties, of the penalty resulting from the commutation and not of the penalty initially imposed.

CHAPTER 2: APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW IN TIME

Article 112-1

A law which removes the punishable nature of an act has retroactive effect. It stops any prosecution in progress as well as the execution of the penalty imposed.
A law which reduces a penalty has retroactive effect. It applies to offences committed before it enters into force and which have not given rise to a conviction which has become final.
A law which makes an act punishable or which increases a penalty has no retroactive effect.

CHAPTER 3: APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW IN SPACE

Article 113-1

Burkinabè criminal law applies to any offence committed on national territory regardless of the nationality of its perpetrator.
Criminal law also applies to offences committed by a national or against a national outside national territory where the acts are punishable under the legislation of the country where they were committed. Prosecution in this case must be preceded by a complaint from the victim or an official denunciation by the authority of the country where the acts were committed.
Prosecution ceases in the case where the person proves that he or she has been finally tried abroad for the same acts, and in the case of conviction, where the penalty has been executed or is time-barred.
Burkinabè criminal law is also applicable to offences committed on board aircraft registered in Burkina Faso, or aircraft leased without crew and operated by persons meeting the conditions to be the owner of an aircraft in Burkina Faso, or against persons on board. It alone is applicable to offences committed on board Burkinabè military aircraft, or against persons on board such aircraft.
It is also applicable to offences committed on board a vessel registered under Burkinabè law or in respect of which a permit or

identification number has been issued in accordance with that law, or on board a vessel registered abroad and belonging to the Burkinabè State or against persons on board such vessels.
For the purposes of this article, Burkinabè courts have jurisdiction.

Article 113-2

Where the extradition, expulsion or refoulement of a person is refused by the Burkinabè authorities to a State where that person faces the risk of being subjected to torture or similar practices, Burkinabè courts have jurisdiction to try the person provided that the acts which are the subject of the request for surrender are provided for and punishable under the legislation in force in Burkina Faso or if they constitute an international crime.

TITLE II

THE OFFENCE AND THE ATTEMPT CHAPTER 1: CLASSIFICATION OF OFFENCES

Article 121-1

Offences punishable by life imprisonment or by imprisonment exceeding ten years are classified as crimes.
Offences punishable by imprisonment of at least thirty days and not exceeding ten years and/or punishable by a fine exceeding two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs are classified as offences.
Offences punishable by a fine not exceeding two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs are classified as contraventions.

CHAPTER 2: THE ATTEMPT

Article 122-1

An attempt consists in the undertaking to commit a crime or an offence, manifested by unequivocal acts tending towards its execution, if those acts were suspended or failed to have their effect only by circumstances independent of the will of their perpetrator.

An attempt is punishable even where the desired result could not be achieved by reason of circumstances unknown to the perpetrator.

Article 122-2

An attempt to commit a crime is always punishable.
An attempt to commit an offence is punishable only in the cases provided for by law. An attempt to commit a contravention is not punishable.

Article 122-3

A preparatory act does not constitute an offence except where otherwise provided by law.

Article 122-4

The penalty applicable to an attempt is that of the offence itself.

TITLE III

CRIMINAL LIABILITY CHAPTER 1: PUNISHABLE PERSONS

Article 131-1

The age of criminal majority is set at eighteen years. It is assessed on the day the acts were committed.
The age of the minor is determined by the production of birth certificates, declaratory judgments or any other documents corroborated by medical expertise. In case of contradiction as to the determination of age, the competent court seized has sovereign discretion.
If the documents produced specify only the year of birth, it shall be considered to have occurred on the thirty-first of December of that year. If the month is specified, the birth shall be considered to have occurred on the last day of that month.

Article 131-2

Any natural person who, personally and in a principal manner, accomplishes the constituent elements of an offence by commission or omission or who is at the origin of such acts is an author or co-author.
Any legal person with a civil, commercial, industrial or financial purpose in whose name and in whose interest acts of execution or abstention constituting an offence have been accomplished by the deliberate will of its organs or representative, in the exercise of their function, is also an author or co-author.

Article 131-3

The State and its subdivisions are also criminally liable for offences committed on their behalf, by their organs or representatives, in the exercise of their function.
Local authorities and their groupings are criminally liable only for offences committed in the exercise of activities that may be the subject of public service delegation agreements.
The criminal liability of legal persons, whether private or public, does not exclude that of natural persons who are authors or accomplices of the same acts, unless the law provides otherwise.

Article 131-4

Any person who is an accomplice to an act qualified as a crime or offence:	anyone who provides weapons, instruments or any other means that were used in the act while knowing that they were to be used for it;	anyone who knowingly prepared or facilitated the consummation of the act, aided or assisted the author or authors of the acts;	anyone who by gift, promise, threat, order, abuse of authority or power provoked the commission of an offence or gave instructions to commit it;
anyone who, knowing the criminal conduct of wrongdoers exercising acts of brigandage or violence against the security of the State, public peace, persons or property, habitually provides them with lodging, a place of retreat or meeting;	anyone who, having knowledge of a crime or offence already attempted or consummated, did not, when it was still possible to prevent or limit its effects or when it could be thought that the guilty parties or one of them would commit new crimes that a denunciation could prevent, immediately notify the administrative or judicial authorities.
Excluded from the cases referred to in dashes 4 and 5 of this article are the spouse, relatives or in-laws of the author of the crime or offence up to and including the fourth degree.

Article 131-5

Accomplices to a crime or offence are punished as the authors of the crime or offence unless the law provides otherwise.

Article 131-6

The authors, co-authors and accomplices of a crime or offence or an attempt to commit a crime or offence are also liable for any other offence whose commission or attempt is a foreseeable consequence of the offence.

Article 131-7

Anyone who causes a non-punishable person to commit an offence is liable to the penalties for the offence committed.

Article 131-8

Anyone who incites the commission of a crime or offence is punished with the penalties provided for the offence even if it was not committed due to the voluntary abstention of the person who was to commit it.

CHAPTER 2: GROUNDS FOR CRIMINAL NON-LIABILITY

Section 1: Justifications Article 132-1

A person who accomplishes an act prescribed or authorized by legislative or regulatory provisions is not criminally liable.
A person who accomplishes an act ordered by legitimate authority is not criminally liable, unless that act is manifestly illegal.

Article 132-2

A person who, faced with an unjustified attack against themselves or others, accomplishes, at the same time, an act commanded by the necessity of self-defence or defence of others, is not criminally liable, unless there is disproportion between the means of defence employed and the seriousness of the attack.
A person who, to interrupt the execution of a crime or offence against property, accomplishes an act of defence, other than voluntary homicide, is not criminally liable when that act is strictly necessary for the purpose pursued provided that the means employed are proportionate to the seriousness of the offence.
Acts committed in the following circumstances are notably commanded by the immediate necessity of self-defence:	to repel, at night, entry by climbing, breaking and entering, violence or ruse into a house, an inhabited apartment or their outbuildings or any other inhabited place;	to defend oneself against the authors of thefts or pillaging executed with violence.

Article 132-3

A person who, faced with a current or imminent danger that threatens themselves, others or property, accomplishes an act necessary for the safeguarding of the person or property, is not criminally liable, unless there is disproportion between the means employed and the seriousness of the threat.

Section 2: Grounds for non-imputability Article 132-4

There is no crime, offence or contravention when the author was in a state of insanity at the time of the act. When the court considers that the mental state of the insane person could compromise public order or the safety of persons, it may order their internment and care in a specialized center. The costs of care are borne in accordance with the health legislation in force.
There is no crime, offence or contravention when the author acted under the influence of a force or constraint that they could not resist.

Article 132-5

A person who was suffering, at the time of the acts, from a psychic or neuropsychic disorder that impaired their judgment or hindered the control of their acts remains punishable; however, the court takes this circumstance into account when it determines the penalty and sets its regime.

Article 132-6

When a public service of the State or a public authority is at the origin of an error of law, a person who proves that they believed, by this error which they were not in a position to avoid, that they could legitimately accomplish the act is not criminally liable.

Article 132-7

The age of criminal responsibility is set at thirteen years.
There is no crime, offence or contravention when the author of the offence was under thirteen years of age on the date the acts were committed.
A minor under thirteen years of age may only be subject to educational and security measures.
Other categories of minors criminally responsible for crimes, offences or contraventions are dealt with under conditions set by a special law that determines the measures of protection, assistance, supervision and education.

Article 132-8

In cases of non-imputability, the courts seized may order all restitutions and pronounce all reparations in accordance with the provisions of the civil code.

BOOK II: PENALTIES, SECURITY MEASURES AND EDUCATIONAL MEASURES

TITLE I: PENALTIES

CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article 211-1

The judge may always impose a firm sentence or a suspended sentence or a mixed sentence.
Suspension consists of a partial or total exemption from execution of a sentence of imprisonment or fine.
Suspension is not applicable to sentences of imprisonment imposed for a duration exceeding five years.
Suspension is revocable under the conditions governed by the code of criminal procedure.
A mixed sentence consists of imposing a sentence of imprisonment or fine of which one part is firm and the other accompanied by suspension.
Conviction to the penalties provided by law is always pronounced without prejudice to restitution and damages that may be owed to the parties.

CHAPTER 2: PENALTIES IN CRIMINAL MATTERS

Section 1: Penalties applicable to adults Article 212-1

Penalties in criminal matters are: life imprisonment; imprisonment for a fixed term; fine for legal persons; dissolution for legal persons.
Criminal penalties do not exclude one or more legally provided complementary penalties or a fine penalty.
The competent jurisdiction may also order the destruction of the thing that was used or intended to commit the offense or of the thing that is the product thereof.

Article 212-2

Conviction to the penalty of imprisonment for a fixed term, in criminal matters, is from more than ten years up to life imprisonment.

Article 212-3

Conviction to a criminal penalty may entail civic degradation.
The civic degradation pronounced runs from the day the conviction became final.

Article 212-4

Civic degradation consists: in the removal and exclusion of convicted persons from all functions, employment or public offices; in the deprivation of the right to vote, eligibility and in general all civic and political rights and the right to wear a decoration;
in the incapacity to be an expert, witness in acts and to testify in court otherwise than to give simple information; in the incapacity to be part of any family council, and to be guardian, curator, deputy guardian or legal adviser, except for one's own children and upon the conforming opinion of the family; in the deprivation of the right to carry weapons, to serve in the national armed forces, to teach or to direct an educational or training establishment, to be employed in an educational institution, as a teacher or supervisor.

Article 212-5

A person convicted to a sentence of life imprisonment may not dispose of his property, in whole or in part, either by inter vivos donation or by will, nor receive it in this capacity, except for maintenance. Any will made by him prior to his adversarial conviction, having become final, is null. The above provisions are not applicable to a person convicted in absentia.
A person convicted to a sentence of life imprisonment may be relieved of all or part of the incapacities contained in the preceding paragraph. He may be granted the exercise, in the place of execution of the sentence, of civil rights or some of these rights, of which he was deprived by his state of legal interdiction. Acts done by the convicted person in the place of execution of the sentence may not encumber the property he possessed on the day of his conviction, or which came to him gratuitously since that time.

Article 212-6

In all cases where a conviction is pronounced for a crime against the external security of the State, committed in time of war, the competent jurisdictions pronounce the confiscation, for the benefit of the nation, of all present property of the convicted person, of whatever nature, movable, immovable, divided or undivided, according to the procedures provided in articles 212-7 and 212-8 below.

Article 212-7

If the convicted person is married, confiscation applies only to the convicted person's share in the division of the community property, or of the undivided property between his spouse and him.
If he has descendants or ascendants, confiscation applies only to the disposable portion. If necessary, division or sale by auction is carried out according to the rules applicable in matters of succession.

Article 212-8

The alienation of confiscated property is pursued by the National Agency for Management and Recovery of Seized or Confiscated Assets in the forms prescribed for the sale of State property.
Property devolved to the State by the effect of confiscation remains encumbered, up to the amount of their value, by legitimate debts prior to the conviction.

Section 2: Penalties applicable to minors Article 212-9

Without prejudice to the application of the provisions of this chapter that are not contrary to this article, a minor over thirteen years of age, found guilty of a crime may be sentenced, by reasoned decision, to a custodial sentence.
When the excuse of minority is retained, the sentence imposed may not exceed half of the sentence provided for adults. In any event, it may not exceed fifteen years.
The measures and sanctions provided in article 213-8 may, where appropriate, be imposed on the minor found guilty of a crime.

CHAPTER 3: PENALTIES IN CORRECTIONAL MATTERS

Section 1: Penalties applicable to adults Article 213-1

Penalties in correctional matters are: imprisonment for a fixed term; fine; community service work; temporary prohibition; permanent prohibition; dissolution for legal persons.
The competent jurisdiction may also order the confiscation and/or destruction of the thing that was used or intended to commit the offense or of the thing that is the product thereof.
In all cases, correctional penalties do not exclude one or more legally provided complementary penalties.

Article 213-2

Dissolution may only be pronounced: when the legal person is legally created; when it concerns a crime or an offense punishable by a sentence of imprisonment greater than or equal to three years for natural persons; when the legal person has been diverted from its purpose to commit the incriminated acts.

Article 213-3

The duration of the sentence of imprisonment, in correctional matters, is at least thirty days and at most ten years, except in cases where the law has determined other limits.
In the sentence of imprisonment, each day counts for twenty-four hours and each month for thirty days.

Article 213-4

Community service work is a penalty that the correctional jurisdiction may impose as a principal penalty when an offense is punishable by a sentence of imprisonment. It consists of having the consenting convicted person perform, for a duration provided by law, unpaid community service work for the benefit of a legal person under public law or a legally recognized non-profit association.
It may not be imposed cumulatively with one of the other penalties provided in article 213-1 above.
Community service work may not be imposed against the accused who refuses it or who is not present at the hearing. The president of the court, before pronouncing the judgment, informs the accused of his right to refuse to perform community service work and receives his response.
The quantum and the procedures for implementing community service work are determined by the law governing the administration of community service work.

Article 213-5

Sentences of community service are expressed in hours or days.
The court that imposes the sentence of community service shall set the period within which the community service must be completed, within a limit of twenty-four months. The period ends upon completion of all the community service; it may be temporarily suspended for serious medical, family, professional or social reasons. This period is suspended during the time the convicted person is placed in pre-trial detention, serves a custodial sentence or fulfills national service obligations.

The terms of execution of the obligation to perform community service and the suspension of the period provided for in the preceding paragraph are decided by the sentence enforcement judge.

Article 213-6

Correctional courts may, in certain cases, prohibit, in whole or in part, the exercise of the following civic, civil and family rights:	the right to vote;	the right to stand for election;	the right to be called or appointed to public functions or positions in the administration, or to exercise such functions or positions;	the right to bear arms;	the right to vote and suffrage in family deliberations;	the right to be a guardian or curator, except in the case of one's own children and only upon the advice of the family;	the right to be an expert or employed as a witness in legal acts;	the right to testify in court, other than to make simple declarations.

Article 213-7

Correctional courts shall only impose the prohibition mentioned in the preceding article when it is authorized or ordered by a specific provision of law.

Section 2: Penalties applicable to minors Article 213-8

Without prejudice to the application of the provisions of this chapter that are not contrary to this article, a minor against whom the commission of an offense is established may be sentenced by reasoned decision to one or more of the following measures:
admonishment;	reprimand;	return to parents, extended family, legal representatives, or a trustworthy person;	placement in an institution, a public or private establishment authorized for education or vocational training;	placement in an institution or a public or private establishment specialized in the reintegration of children in conflict with the law through education or vocational training;	placement in a medical or medico-educational establishment, if necessary.
A minor over the age of thirteen may also be sentenced to a fine or to imprisonment for a fixed term.
A minor over the age of sixteen may be sentenced to community service, under the conditions provided for in this code.

CHAPTER 4: OTHER PENALTIES INCURRED FOR CRIMES AND OFFENSES

Article 214-1

When the law so provides, a legal person, prosecuted for a crime or offense, may, in addition to the principal penalty, be sanctioned with one or more of the following penalties:	the prohibition, permanently or for a period of up to five years, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more professional or social activities;	placement, for a period of up to five years, under judicial supervision;	the permanent closure or closure for a period of up to five years of all establishments or one or more establishments of the enterprise that were used to commit the incriminated acts;
exclusion from public contracts and public service delegations permanently or for a period of up to five years;	the prohibition, permanently or for a period of up to five years, from making a public offer of financial securities or from having its financial securities admitted to trading on a regulated market;	the prohibition, for a period of up to five years, from issuing checks other than those that allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those that are certified, or from using payment cards;	the penalty of confiscation of all or part of its property;	publication of the decision rendered, either in the print press, or by any means of communication to the public including electronically, or posting thereof.

Article 214-2

Banishment from residence, fines and special confiscation, either of the corpus delicti when the property belongs to the convicted person, or of things produced by the offense, or of those that were used or intended to commit it, are penalties common to criminal and correctional matters.

Article 214-3

The duration of any custodial sentence runs from the date on which the conviction decision becomes final.

Article 214-4

In the case of a sentence to imprisonment for a fixed term, pre-trial detention is credited day for day against the duration of the sentence imposed by the judgment or conviction order.

Article 214-5

Sentences of imprisonment are served in penitentiary establishments provided for that purpose.

Article 214-6

The prohibition from exercising a public function or a professional or social activity is either permanent or temporary. In the latter case, it may not exceed ten years.
This prohibition does not apply to the exercise of an elective mandate or trade union responsibility. Nor does it apply in matters of press offenses.

Article 214-7

The penalty of exclusion from public contracts and public service delegations entails the prohibition from participating, directly or indirectly, in any contract concluded by the State and its public establishments, territorial authorities, their groupings and their public establishments, as well as by enterprises granted concessions or controlled by the State or by territorial authorities or their groupings.
The penalty of exclusion is permanent or temporary. In the latter case, it may not exceed five years.

Article 214-8

The penalty of closure of an establishment entails the prohibition from exercising the activity in connection with which the offense was committed.

Article 214-9

Anyone who violates the penalty of prohibition from exercising a commercial or industrial profession, from directing, administering, managing or controlling in any capacity, directly or indirectly, on their own account or on behalf of others, a commercial or industrial enterprise or a commercial company, imposed against them, is punished by imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 214-10

Banishment from residence consists of the prohibition imposed on a convicted person from appearing in certain places.

It also includes measures of supervision and assistance.
Its duration is from two to ten years in correctional matters, from eleven to thirty years in criminal matters.
It may be imposed against:	anyone sentenced to imprisonment for a crime;	anyone sentenced for a crime or offense against the internal or external security of the State;	anyone who, having been sentenced to a term of imprisonment exceeding one year, has, within five years after the expiration of that sentence or its prescription, been sentenced to a term equal to or exceeding one year of imprisonment.

Article 214-11

Any person sentenced to life imprisonment who obtains a commutation or remission of sentence is, unless otherwise provided by the clemency decision, subject by operation of law to banishment from residence for five years.
The same applies to any person sentenced to life imprisonment whose sentence is time-barred.

Article 214-12

A ban from the territory may be pronounced for a period greater than or equal to ten years or permanently, against any foreigner guilty of a felony or misdemeanor.
The convicted person is escorted to the border by operation of law, upon expiration of his or her prison sentence, where applicable.

Article 214-13

The list of prohibited places is established by the minister in charge of territorial administration, by individual order issued jointly with the minister in charge of justice.

The same order determines the surveillance and assistance measures to which the convicted person may be subjected.

Article 214-14

The prohibition order is notified to the convicted person who receives, in addition to an anthropometric booklet, the legal identity card. The decisions or orders issued pursuant to Article 214-13 above are also notified to him or her.
If notification of the prohibition order was made to the convicted person before his or her final or conditional release, the prohibition runs from the date of that release. However, in case of revocation of conditional release, the prohibition is suspended during the time of the new incarceration. The same applies in the case of detention for any other cause.
If the prohibition order could not be notified to him or her before his or her release, the convicted person must, at that time, inform the director or chief guard of the penitentiary establishment where he or she was detained of the place where he or she intends to establish his or her residence. He or she is further required, during the three months following his or her release, to notify him of any change of that residence, and to respond to the summons addressed to him or her by the administrative authority for the purpose of notification of the prohibition order.
If he or she complies with these obligations, the prohibition runs from the date of release; otherwise, it takes effect only from the day when notification of the prohibition order could be made to him or her.
If no custodial sentence without suspension has been pronounced or if that sentence has expired, notification of the prohibition order is made to the convicted person as soon as the judgment or decision imposing the residence ban has become final; the prohibition runs from the day when the judgment or decision acquired that character.
In the case of a sentence of life imprisonment, the residence ban takes effect from the day when the prescription period is completed.

Article 214-15

Any person banned from residence who, in violation of the order notified to him or her, appears in a place prohibited to him or her is punished by imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000)

CFA francs.
Any person who evades the surveillance measures prescribed by the order notified to him or her, or who fails to respond to the summons addressed to him or her by the administrative authority for the purpose of notification of the prohibition order in the case provided for in paragraph 3 of Article 214-14 above, is punished by the same penalties.

Article 214-16

Joint orders issued by the ministers in charge of justice and territorial administration determine the conditions for application of Articles 214-10, 214-12, and 214-13 above.
They establish, in particular: the surveillance and assistance measures that may be prescribed pursuant to Articles 214-10 and 214-13 above; the conditions for establishment and issuance and the modalities of the documents provided for in Article 214-14, paragraph 1 above; the entries and endorsements to be made on the anthropometric booklet, the form of notifications of the orders provided for in Articles 214-13 and 214-14 and of the summons provided for in Article 214-14 paragraph 3 above.

Article 214-17

In cases specifically provided for by law, the courts seized may order the posting of their decision, the content of which is set in very conspicuous characters, in the places they indicate, at the expense of the convicted person.
Unless otherwise provided by law, such posting is ordered for a period that may not exceed two months in the case of felonies or misdemeanors.
The total or partial removal, concealment and tearing of posters affixed in accordance with this article, carried out voluntarily by the convicted person, at his or her instigation or on his or her orders, are punished by imprisonment of one month to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs. The posting is carried out again in full at the expense of the convicted person.

The courts seized may order the dissemination of all or part of their decision and determine the newspaper(s) or audiovisual communication service responsible for such dissemination.
The posting or dissemination may include the name of the victim only with his or her consent or that of his or her legal representative.

Article 214-18

If restitution is required, the guilty party may be further condemned, toward the injured party, if the latter so requests, to damages the determination of which is left to the discretion of the court seized.

Article 214-19

The judge, for serious medical, family, professional or social reasons, may decide to allow payment of the fine in installments.
For the same reasons and in the case where the judgment did not provide for it, the same court may, upon application by the convicted person, order payment of the fine in installments.
In case of insufficient assets, restitution and damages owed to the injured party take precedence over the fine.

Article 214-20

Enforcement of sentences to fines and costs owed to the Public Treasury may be pursued by means of imprisonment for debt.

Article 214-21

When fines and costs are pronounced in favor of the State, if, after expiration of the criminal sentence, the imprisonment of the convicted person for payment of these pecuniary sentences has lasted a full year, he or she may, upon proof established by legal means of his or her absolute insolvency, obtain provisional release.
The duration of imprisonment is reduced to six months if it concerns a misdemeanor; subject, in all cases, to resumption of imprisonment for debt if the convicted person acquires any means of solvency.

Article 214-22

All persons convicted of the same felony or the same misdemeanor are jointly and severally liable for fines, restitution, damages and costs.

Article 214-23

The complementary penalty of confiscation is incurred in cases provided for by law or regulation. It is also incurred automatically for crimes and for offences punishable by a term of imprisonment exceeding one year, with the exception of press offences.
Confiscation applies to all movable or immovable property, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, which was used to commit the offence or which was intended to be used to commit it, and of which the convicted person is the owner or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal.
It also applies to all property which is the object or the direct or indirect proceeds of the offence, with the exception of property capable of being returned to the victim. If the proceeds of the offence have been mixed with funds of lawful origin for the acquisition of one or more properties, confiscation may apply to such property only up to the estimated value of such proceeds.
Confiscation may also apply to any movable or immovable property defined by the law or regulation which punishes the offence.
If it is a crime or an offence punishable by at least five years' imprisonment and having procured a direct or indirect profit, confiscation also applies to movable or immovable property, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal, when neither the convicted person nor the owner, given the opportunity to explain the property whose confiscation is contemplated, has been able to justify its origin.
When the law which punishes the crime or offence so provides, confiscation may also apply to all or part of the property belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal, whatever its nature, movable or immovable, divided or undivided.

Confiscation is mandatory for objects classified as dangerous or harmful by law or regulation, or the possession of which is unlawful, whether or not such property is the property of the convicted person.
The complementary penalty of confiscation applies under the same conditions to all incorporeal rights, whatever their nature, divided or undivided.
Confiscation may be ordered by value. Confiscation by value may be executed on all property, whatever its nature, belonging to the convicted person or over which he has free disposal, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith. For the recovery of the sum representing the value of the confiscated thing, the provisions relating to judicial constraint are applicable.
The confiscated thing is, unless a special provision provides for its destruction or allocation, transferred to the State, but it remains encumbered, up to its value, with real rights lawfully created in favour of third parties.
When the confiscated thing is a vehicle which has not been seized or impounded during the proceedings, the convicted person must, upon the injunction given to him by the public prosecutor, hand over this vehicle to the service or body responsible for its destruction or disposal.

CHAPITRE 5 : DES PEINES EN MATIERE CONTRAVENTIONNELLE

Article 215-1

The penalties in matters of contraventions are: the fine; the complementary penalties legally provided for in matters of contraventions.

CHAPITRE 6 : DE LA PERSONNALISATION DES PEINES ET DES CIRCONSTANCES AGGRAVANTES

Article 216-1

Within the limits fixed by law, the court pronounces the penalties and fixes their regime according to the circumstances of the offence and the personality of its perpetrator.
Personal circumstances from which result an exoneration of responsibility, an exemption, a mitigation or an aggravation of penalty have effect only with regard to the person who is the subject thereof.

Article 216-2

Organised gang, assembly, ambush, premeditation, breaking and entering, scaling, use of a weapon, use of a means of cryptology in particular constitute aggravating circumstances in the cases provided for by this code.
The status of spouse, that of father or mother, that of cohabitant, that of child may also constitute aggravating circumstances.
Objective circumstances inherent in the offence which aggravate or diminish penalties have effect with regard to the co-perpetrator or accomplice only if he could foresee them.

Article 216-3

An organised gang within the meaning of the law constitutes any group formed or any agreement established with a view to the preparation, characterised by one or more material facts, of one or more offences.

Article 216-4

An assembly within the meaning of the law constitutes any collective action of at least two persons, occasional or fortuitous, on the occasion of the commission of one or more offences.

Article 216-5

Premeditation consists in the design formed before the action to make an attempt on the person of a determined individual or even of one who will be found or encountered, even though this design may be dependent on some circumstance or some condition.

Article 216-6

Ambush consists in waiting, for a certain time in one or various places, for an individual either to put him to death or to exercise acts of violence on him.

Article 216-7

Breaking and entering consists in the forcing, degradation or destruction of any closing device or any kind of enclosure.
Assimilated to breaking and entering is the use of false keys, keys unduly obtained or any instrument that can be fraudulently used to operate a closing device without forcing or degrading it.

Article 216-8

Scaling is the act of entering any place, either over an element of enclosure or through any opening not intended to serve as an entrance.

Article 216-9

A weapon is any object or device designed or intended to kill, injure, strike, neutralise or to cause incapacity.
Also considered as a weapon for the application of this code, in addition to firearms and objects considered as weapons by the texts in force, any object, instrument, tool or utensil that is cutting, piercing or blunt, hand-held or thrown.
Scissors, pocket knives and simple canes are deemed to be weapons only if they are used to kill, injure, strike or threaten.
Assimilated to a weapon is any object which, presenting with the weapon defined in paragraph 1 a resemblance of a nature to create confusion, is used to threaten to kill or injure or is intended, by the person carrying it, to threaten to kill or injure.
The use of an animal to kill, injure or threaten is assimilated to the use of a weapon.

Article 216-10

A cryptology means is any hardware or software designed or modified to transform data, whether information or signals, using secret conventions or to perform the reverse operation with or without secret conventions.
These cryptology means have as their principal purpose to guarantee the security of data storage or transmission, by enabling their confidentiality, authentication or integrity control to be ensured.
A cryptology service is any operation aimed at implementing, on behalf of others, cryptology means.
When a cryptology means has been used to prepare or commit a crime or offence, or to facilitate its preparation or commission, the maximum custodial sentence incurred is increased as follows:	it is increased to life imprisonment when the offence is punishable by twenty years' imprisonment;	it is increased to twenty years' imprisonment when the offence is punishable by less than twenty years and more than ten years' imprisonment;	it is increased to ten years' imprisonment when the offence is punishable by more than three years and less than ten years' imprisonment;	it is increased to double when the offence is punishable by three years' imprisonment at most.
The provisions of this article are not, however, applicable to the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence who, at the request of the judicial or administrative authorities, has provided them with the plain version of encrypted messages as well as the secret conventions necessary for decryption.

Article 216-11

When it concerns the cases respectively provided for by law or regulation, the penalties incurred for a crime, offence or contravention are aggravated when the offence is committed by the spouse or cohabitant.
The aggravating circumstance provided for in the first paragraph is also constituted when the acts are committed by the former spouse, the former cohabitant. The provisions of this paragraph are applicable where the offence is committed by reason of the relations having existed between the perpetrator of the acts and the victim.
In the cases provided for by law, the penalties incurred for a crime or offence are aggravated when the offence is committed by reason of the membership or non-membership, real or supposed, of the victim in a particular ethnicity, nation, race or religion.
The aggravating circumstance defined in the first paragraph is constituted when the offence is preceded, accompanied or followed by words, writings, images, objects or acts of any nature damaging the honour or consideration of the victim or of a group of persons of which the victim is part by reason of their membership or non-membership, real or supposed, in a particular ethnicity, nation, race or religion.

CHAPITRE 7 : DES EXCUSES ET DES CIRCONSTANCES ATTENUANTES

Article 217-1

No crime or offence may be excused, nor the penalty mitigated, except in the cases and circumstances where the law declares the act excusable or permits a less severe penalty to be applied to it.

Article 217-2

Absolutory excuses ensure impunity for the perpetrator of the offence; however, educational or security measures may be applied to him.
Mitigating excuses ensure for the guilty party a moderation of the penalty. The minority of the perpetrator at the time of the acts is a mitigating excuse.

The court seised of the merits may disregard the excuse of minority for minors aged sixteen years or more.

Article 217-3

When the excuse is admitted by the court seised, the applicable penalties are as follows:	imprisonment of one year to five years, if the penalty incurred is life imprisonment;	imprisonment of six months to two years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of five years to ten years or eleven years to twenty-one years;	imprisonment of thirty days to six months, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of two months to five years.

Article 217-4

Unless otherwise provided by law, if the court seised recognises by reasoned decision mitigating circumstances for the guilty party which, without being excuses, are nevertheless of a nature to mitigate his criminal or delictual responsibility, the penalties are modified as follows:	imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years, if the penalty incurred is life imprisonment;	imprisonment of five years to ten years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of eleven to thirty years;	imprisonment of two years to five years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of five years to ten years.
In correctional matters, the court which recognises mitigating circumstances is authorised to reduce the imprisonment and the fine even below the penalties for simple police offences.
If the law provides for the cumulative application of imprisonment and a fine, the court may impose both penalties by reducing them or by reducing only one of them. If the law provides for a penalty of imprisonment alone, the court may substitute a fine for it; the substitution fine is five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs.

CHAPITRE 8 : DE LA RECIDIVE

Article 218-1

In the cases limitatively enumerated by law, the penalties incurred for crimes and offences are aggravated in case of recidivism.

Article 218-2

In criminal and delictual matters, is a recidivist anyone who, after having been definitively convicted for a first offence by a national or foreign court, subject to the offence having motivated the conviction abroad also being an offence under national law, commits a second crime or a second offence independent of the first offence.

Article 218-3

May be sentenced to double the penalties provided for the second offence anyone who having:	already been definitively convicted for a crime, commits a new crime;	already been definitively convicted for a crime, commits within the period of five years from the expiry or prescription of the penalty, an intentional offence;	already been definitively convicted for an intentional offence, commits within the period of five years, from the expiry or prescription of the penalty, a crime;	already been definitively convicted for an offence, commits within the period of five years from the expiry or prescription of the penalty, an identical or assimilated offence;
already been definitively convicted for a contravention, commits within the period of one year from the expiry or prescription of the penalty, the same contravention.

Article 218-4

The offences of theft, fraud, breach of trust, abuse of blank signature, receiving stolen goods, misappropriation of public funds or seized objects, extortion of funds, swindling, corruption, extortion, offences relating to cheques and offences in company matters are considered as the same offence from the point of view of recidivism.

Article 218-5

In contravention matters, there is recidivism when a first judgment that has become final has been rendered against the offender in the preceding twelve months.

Article 218-6

In case of concurrence of aggravating circumstances and mitigating circumstances, the rules of mitigating circumstances are applied to the penalty resulting from the aggravating circumstances.
In case of concurrence of mitigating circumstances and a legal excuse, the rules of mitigating circumstances are applied to the penalty resulting from the admission of the excuse.
If mitigating circumstances are admitted for a recidivist, the penalty resulting from the recidivism must first be fixed, before reducing it by reason of the mitigating circumstances.

CHAPTER 9: EXTINCTION AND ERASURE OF PENALTY

Article 219-1

A person who has attempted to commit a crime or an offence is, in cases provided for by law, exempt from penalty if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, they have made it possible to prevent the commission of the offence and, where applicable, to identify the co-authors or accomplices.

In cases provided for by law, the duration of the custodial sentence incurred by a person who has committed a crime or an offence is reduced if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, they have made it possible to bring the offence to an end, to prevent the offence from causing harm or to identify the other authors or accomplices.
The provisions of the preceding paragraph are also applicable when the person has made it possible either to prevent the commission of a related offence of the same nature as the crime or offence for which they were prosecuted, or to bring such an offence to an end, to prevent it from causing harm or to identify its authors or accomplices.

Article 219-2

Penalties imposed for a crime are prescribed after twenty full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final, unless the law provides otherwise.

Article 219-3

Penalties imposed for an offence are prescribed after five full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final.

Article 219-4

Penalties imposed for a contravention are prescribed after three full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final.

Article 219-5

Civil obligations arising from a final criminal decision are prescribed according to the rules of the civil code.

Article 219-6

Pardon only entails dispensation from executing the penalty.
Pardon does not prevent the victim's right to obtain reparation for the harm caused by the offence.

Article 219-7

Amnesty erases the convictions pronounced. It entails, without giving rise to restitution, the remission of all penalties. It restores the author or accomplice of the offence to the benefit of the suspended sentence that may have been granted to them upon a previous conviction.
Amnesty does not prejudice the rights of third parties.

Article 219-8

Any person who, in the exercise of their functions, has knowledge of criminal convictions, disciplinary or professional sanctions or prohibitions, disqualifications and incapacities erased by amnesty, is prohibited from recalling their existence in any form whatsoever or from allowing mention of them to remain in any document.
However, the minutes of judgments, rulings and decisions are exempt from this prohibition. Furthermore, amnesty does not prevent the execution of publication ordered by way of reparation.

Article 219-9

Any person subject to a criminal, correctional or contravention penalty may benefit either from automatic rehabilitation under the conditions provided for in this section, or from judicial rehabilitation granted under the conditions provided for by the code of criminal procedure.

Article 219-10

Rehabilitation is automatically acquired by a natural person convicted who has not, within the time limits determined below, incurred any new conviction to a criminal or correctional penalty:	for conviction to a fine after a period of three years from the day of payment of the fine, the expiry of judicial constraint or the completion of prescription;	for a single conviction either to imprisonment not exceeding one year, or to a penalty other than life imprisonment,

fixed-term imprisonment, fine, after a period of five years from either the execution of the penalty or the completion of prescription;	for a single conviction to imprisonment not exceeding ten years or for multiple convictions to imprisonment the total of which does not exceed five years, after a period of ten years from either the expiry of the penalty served or the completion of prescription.
The time limits provided for in this article are doubled when the person has been convicted for acts committed in a state of legal recidivism.
In the case of convictions accompanied in whole or in part by a suspended sentence, the rehabilitation time limits run, for each of these convictions and including in the case of multiple convictions, from the date on which the conviction becomes null and void.

Article 219-11

Rehabilitation is automatically acquired by a legal person convicted who has not, within the time limits determined below, incurred any new conviction to a criminal or correctional penalty:	for conviction to a fine, after a period of five years from the day of payment of the fine or the completion of prescription;	for conviction to a penalty other than a fine or dissolution, after a period of five years from either the execution of the penalty or the completion of prescription.
The time limits provided for in this article are doubled when the person has been convicted for acts committed in a state of legal recidivism.
In the case of a conviction accompanied by a suspended sentence, the rehabilitation time limits run from the date on which the conviction becomes null and void.

Article 219-12

Penalties for which merger has been granted are considered as constituting a single penalty for the application of the provisions of the two preceding articles.

Article 219-13

Rehabilitation produces the same effects as those provided for by the articles relating to amnesty and pardon.
It erases all incapacities and disqualifications resulting from the conviction.
However, when the person has been sentenced to socio-judicial supervision or to the penalty of prohibition from exercising a professional or voluntary activity involving habitual contact with minors, rehabilitation only produces its effects at the end of the measure.
Rehabilitation does not prohibit the taking into account of the conviction, by the judicial authorities alone, in the event of new prosecutions, for the application of the rules on legal recidivism.

TITLE II

SECURITY MEASURES AND EDUCATIONAL MEASURES CHAPTER 1: SECURITY MEASURES

Article 221-1

Security measures are individual coercive measures imposed on individuals dangerous to the social order in order to prevent the offences that their condition makes probable.
The following constitute security measures:	confinement of the insane;	treatment of persons addicted to narcotics;	treatment of dangerous alcoholics;	measures	concerning	certain	persons	suffering	from	mental or neuropsychiatric disorder;	supervised liberty of minors.

CHAPTER 2: EDUCATIONAL MEASURES

Article 222-1

Educational measures are individual measures that aim at education, re-education, guardianship or assistance in favour of the convicted person.
Educational measures are applicable to minors under eighteen years of age found guilty of crimes or offences.
These are:	return of the minor to their family;	placement of the minor with a relative or a trustworthy person;	placement of the minor in a charitable, religious or private institution;	placement of the minor in a specialised public establishment.

BOOK III

CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE PUBLIC INTEREST TITLE I: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST STATE SECURITY CHAPTER 1: TREASON AND ESPIONAGE

Article 311-1

Is guilty of treason and punished by life imprisonment, any Burkinabè who: bears arms against the State; maintains intelligence with a foreign power with a view to inducing it to undertake hostilities against Burkina Faso or provides it with the means to do so, either by facilitating the penetration of foreign forces into Burkinabè territory, or by undermining the loyalty of the armed forces, or in any other manner; delivers to a foreign power or its agents, either troops, or territories, towns, works, posts, stores, arsenals, buildings,

materials, munitions, ships, aircraft or railway locomotives belonging to Burkina Faso or assigned to its defence; with a view to harming national defence, destroys or damages a ship, an aircraft or railway locomotive, material, supplies, construction or any installation whatsoever, or who, with the same aim, introduces into them, either before or after their completion, defects likely to damage them, to prevent them from functioning normally or to cause an accident.

Article 311-2

Is guilty of treason and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment, any Burkinabè who, in time of war: incites military personnel to enter the service of a foreign power or facilitates the means for them to do so; carries out enlistments for a foreign power; maintains intelligence with a foreign power or with an agent, with a view to favouring the enterprises of that foreign power against Burkina Faso; obstructs the movement of military means or materials; knowingly participates in an enterprise of demoralisation of the armed forces or populations having as its object to harm national defence.

Article 311-3

Is guilty of treason and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment any Burkinabè who, with a view to favouring a foreign power, obtains, delivers, destroys or allows to be destroyed in any form or by any means whatsoever any information, object, document or process which must be kept secret in the interest of national defence.

Article 311-4

Is guilty of espionage and punished by a sentence of imprisonment of thirty years, any foreigner or stateless person who commits one of the acts provided for in articles 311-1 and 311-3 above.
Is guilty of espionage and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment, any foreigner or stateless person who commits one of the acts provided for in article 311-2 above.

Article 311-5

Is punished, as a crime, the provocation to commit or the offer to commit one of the crimes referred to in this chapter.

Article 311-6

Is punished, in time of war, by a sentence of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to eight million (8,000,000) CFA francs and in time of peace, by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to four million (4,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, having complete knowledge of a plan or date of treason or espionage, as to the nature of which he could not be mistaken, does not report it to the administrative, military or judicial authorities, from the moment he became aware of them.

Article 311-7

Is punished by the same penalties, anyone who, being in contact with an individual exercising an activity likely to harm the security of the State, does not notify the authorities referred to in the preceding article from the moment he was able to become aware of that activity.

Article 311-8

Are exempted from the penalties provided for against the perpetrators of conspiracies or other crimes against the security of the State, those perpetrators who, before any execution or attempted execution of these conspiracies or these crimes and before any proceedings commenced, are the first to give knowledge of them to the government, to the administrative or judicial police authorities or who,

even since the commencement of proceedings, facilitate the arrest of said perpetrators and accomplices.

CHAPTER 2: OTHER OFFENCES AGAINST NATIONAL DEFENCE

Article 312-1

Is punished by life imprisonment, anyone who gathers, with the intention of delivering them to a foreign power, information, objects, documents or processes whose collection and exploitation are likely to harm national defence.

Article 312-2

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, any guardian, any custodian by function or by capacity of information, an object, document, or process which must be kept secret in the interest of national defence or whose knowledge may lead to the discovery of a national defence secret who, through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention or non-observance of regulations: destroys it, removes it, allows it to be destroyed or removed, reproduces it or allows it to be reproduced; brings it or allows it to be brought to the knowledge of the public or of an unqualified person.

Article 312-3

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, for a purpose other than that of delivering it to a foreign power or its agents: commits one of the offences provided for in article 312-2 above; secures possession of information, an object, document or process kept secret in the interest of national defence or whose knowledge may lead to the discovery of a military secret.

Article 312-4

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, knowingly and without prior authorisation from the competent authority, delivers or communicates to a person acting on behalf of a foreign power or enterprise, either an invention concerning national defence, or information, studies or manufacturing processes relating to an invention of this kind or to an industrial application concerning national defence.

Article 312-5

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, without intention of treason or espionage, brings to the knowledge of an unqualified person or of the public, information not made public by the competent authority and whose disclosure is manifestly likely to harm national defence.

Article 312-6

Are notably deemed to be national defence secrets for the application of this code: information of a military, diplomatic, economic or industrial nature which, by its nature, must be known only to persons qualified to hold it and must, in the interest of national defence, be kept secret from any other person; objects, materials, writings, drawings, plans, maps, surveys, photographs or other productions and, all other documents whatsoever which, by their nature, must be known only to persons qualified to handle or hold them, and must be kept secret from any other person as being able to lead to the discovery of information belonging to one of the categories referred to above; military information of any nature not made public by the government and not included in the enumerations above whose

publication, dissemination, disclosure or reproduction has been prohibited by a law; information relating either to measures taken to discover and arrest the perpetrators and accomplices of crimes or offences against the external security of the State, or to the progress of prosecutions and investigation, or to debates before the trial court.

Article 312-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years, and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who: enters under a disguise or a false name or by concealing their status or nationality into a work, post or arsenal, into the works, camps, bivouacs or cantonments of an army, into a warship or commercial vessel employed by national defence, into a military vehicle, into an establishment or worksite working for national defence; organizes, even without disguise or without concealing their name, status or nationality, in a clandestine manner, any means of correspondence or remote transmission likely to harm national defence; voluntarily overflies Burkinabè territory by means of a foreign aircraft without being authorized to do so by an agreement or by the Burkinabè authority; executes in a prohibited zone fixed by the military authority without authorization from the latter, drawings, photographs, surveys or topographical operations inside or around places, works, posts or establishments of interest to national defence; stays in defiance of a prohibition within a determined radius around the works listed in the preceding points; communicates to an unqualified person or makes public information relating either to measures taken to discover or arrest the perpetrators and accomplices of crimes or offences defined in chapters 1

and 2 of this title, or to the conduct of the proceedings, or to the debates or deliberations before the trial courts.
However, in peacetime, the offences provided for in bullets 3, 4, 5 and 6 of this article are punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs.

Article 312-8

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who: by hostile acts not approved by the competent authority, exposes Burkina Faso to a declaration of war or to reprisals; by acts not approved by the competent authority, exposes Burkinabè to suffer reprisals; maintains with the agents of a foreign power intelligence likely to harm the military or diplomatic situation of Burkina Faso or its essential economic interests.

Article 312-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in time of war, directly or through an intermediary and in defiance of enacted prohibitions, engages in acts of commerce or maintains correspondence or relations that may harm national defence with subjects or agents of an enemy power.

Article 312-10

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in peacetime, obstructs the movement of military means or materials or by any means whatsoever, provokes, facilitates or organizes a violent or concerted action with a view to harming national defence.

Article 312-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in peacetime, knowingly participates in an enterprise of demoralization of the Armed Forces likely to harm national defence.

Article 312-12

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in peacetime, in Burkina Faso and clandestinely, enlists or trains with a view to their enlistment persons called to bear arms on behalf of a foreign power or organized group.

Article 312-13

The attempt of the offences provided for in this section is punishable.

CHAPITRE 3

DES	ATTENTATS,	DES	COMPLOTS	ET	AUTRES INFRACTIONS CONTRE LA SURETE DE L'ÉTAT

Article 313-1

There is a conspiracy as soon as the resolution to act is concerted and settled between two or more persons with a view to: changing the legal regime by violence; inciting the populations to arm themselves against the legal authority of the State or to arm themselves against one another; undermining the integrity of the national territory; organizing massacre and devastation.
The applicable penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years.

Article 313-2

A conspiracy followed by an act committed or begun to prepare its execution is an attack punished by imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years.

Article 313-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who makes an unagreed proposal to form a conspiracy to commit one of the crimes provided for in article 313-1 above.

Article 313-4

In all cases, the court seized may in addition pronounce the deprivation of civil rights for a period that may not exceed five years.

Article 313-5

Is punished by life imprisonment, anyone who, without having been legally invested or who, without legitimate reason, takes any military command whatsoever or who, against the order of the legitimate authority, retains such command.
Commanders who keep their army or troop assembled after disbandment or separation has been ordered, are punished by the same penalty.

Article 313-6

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in time of war, being able to dispose of the public force, requisitions or orders, causes to be requisitioned or ordered the action or use thereof to prevent the execution of texts on military recruitment or on mobilization.
If this requisition or this order is carried out, the offender is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years.
Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years, and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in peacetime, being able to dispose of the public force requisitions or orders, causes to be requisitioned or ordered the action or use thereof to prevent the execution of texts on military recruitment or on mobilization.

If this requisition or this order is carried out, the offender is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

CHAPITRE 4 : DES CRIMES COMMIS PAR LA PARTICIPATION A UN MOUVEMENT DE DESTABILISATION

Article 314-1

Are punished by imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, those who, in an insurrectionary movement: are found carrying weapons and ammunition; occupy or attempt to occupy public buildings or private properties; erect barricades; oppose by violence and threats the convening or assembly of the public force; provoke or facilitate the gathering of insurgents by flags, rallying signs or any other means; intercept or attempt to intercept communications between the depositaries of the public force; break or attempt to break communication and telecommunication lines; seize weapons and ammunition by violence, or threat, pillage, disarmament of agents of the public force.
Are punished by life imprisonment, those who use their firearms.

Article 314-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs,

anyone who sets fire to or destroys by explosive device buildings, warehouses, arsenals or other properties belonging to the State.
If death has resulted, the perpetrator is punished by a term of life imprisonment.

Article 314-3

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment, anyone who, whether to invade domains or properties of the State, towns, posts, warehouses, arsenals, or to plunder and share public funds, public or national properties or those of a community, or to make attack or resistance against the public force acting against the perpetrators of these crimes, places himself at the head of armed bands or exercises therein any command function whatsoever.
The same penalty applies to those who direct the association, raise or cause to be raised, organize or cause to be organized the bands or provide or procure for them knowingly arms, ammunition and instruments of crime or send convoys of supplies or practice in any other manner intelligence with the leaders of the bands.

Article 314-4

In the case where one or more of the crimes mentioned in this section have been executed or simply attempted by a band, life imprisonment applies to all individuals forming part of the band and who have been seized at the place of the seditious assembly.
Is punished by the same penalty, although not seized at the place, anyone who has directed the sedition or has exercised in the band any command whatsoever.

Article 314-5

No penalty is pronounced for act of sedition against those who, having formed part of these bands without exercising a command therein, withdraw at the first warning of the civil, military authorities or even those who have been seized outside the places of the seditious assembly without offering resistance and without arms.

CHAPITRE 5 : DES ATTROUPEMENTS

Article 315-1

Are prohibited, the formation of armed assemblies on the public highway as well as unarmed assemblies which are of a nature to disturb the public tranquility.
The assembly is deemed armed when several of the individuals who compose it are carrying apparent or concealed weapons or when a single one of these individuals, carrying apparent weapons, is not immediately expelled from the assembly by those very persons who form part of it.

Article 315-2

All persons who form assemblies in squares or on the public highway are required to disperse at the summons or summonses of the authorities responsible for maintaining order.
If the assembly does not disperse, the summons is renewed twice.
If the three summonses have remained without effect or even in the case where after a first summons or a second, it is not possible to make the second or the third, force may be employed.

Article 315-3

The public force may be employed without summonses:	if violence or acts of force are exercised against the forces of order;	if the forces of order cannot otherwise defend the ground they occupy or the persons and posts of which they have custody.

Article 315-4

Anyone who having formed part of an armed assembly which disperses upon the customary summonses, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

If the assembly is formed at night, the term of imprisonment is one year to three years and the fine five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.
Nevertheless, no penalty is pronounced for assembly against those who, having formed part of it without being personally armed, withdraw upon the first summons.

Article 315-5

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who forms part of an armed assembly which disperses only after employment of the public force.
The term of imprisonment is five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to four million (4,000,000) CFA francs, if the armed assembly has been dispersed only after the public force has made use of its weapons.

Article 315-6

The aggravation of penalties provided for in article 315-5 above is not applicable to unarmed individuals forming part of an armed assembly, in the case of concealed weapons, except when they have had knowledge of the presence in the assembly of several persons carrying these weapons.
Those who have not had this knowledge incur the penalties provided for in article 315-4 above.

Article 315-7

In the cases provided for in article 315-5 above, the court seized may in addition pronounce the prohibition from exercising civil rights for a duration which cannot exceed five years.

Article 315-8

Is punished as the crime or the offense according to the distinctions established in the preceding articles, any provocation followed by effect, to an armed or unarmed assembly

by speeches uttered publicly or by writings or printed matter posted or distributed.
Printers, engravers, lithographers, bill-posters and distributors are punished as accomplices, when they act knowingly.
Provocation not followed by effect is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months.

Article 315-9

Prosecutions brought for crimes and offense of assembly do not constitute an obstacle to the prosecution of crimes and offenses which are committed in the midst of these assemblies.

Article 315-10

In all cases, the confiscation of weapons and ammunition must be pronounced.

CHAPITRE 6 : DES ARMES ET MUNITIONS

Article 316-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, without legally required authorization, manufactures, exports, imports, possesses, transfers, sells or purchases a firearm or ammunition or acts as an intermediary in the sale or purchase or is responsible for the transport, transit on the territory of Burkina Faso or transhipment of a firearm or ammunition.

Article 316-2

Is considered as an accomplice anyone who lends a firearm or ammunition to a person without ensuring that the latter is authorized to possess a firearm or ammunition.

Article 316-3

In all cases, the confiscation of the weapon or ammunition is mandatory.
In case of recidivism, the court may pronounce the closure of the incriminated establishment either temporarily for a duration which cannot exceed five years, or definitively.

Article 316-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, anyone who, even having an authorization to carry a weapon, carries a weapon in a place open to the public and in conditions likely to disturb the public peace and to intimidate others.

TITRE II : DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA CONSTITUTION ET LES LIBERTES PUBLIQUES

CHAPITRE 1 : DE LA HAUTE TRAHISON ET DE L'ATTENTAT A LA CONSTITUTION

Article 321-1

There is high treason when the President of the Faso violates his oath, performs acts contrary to the dignity of his office, is author, co-author or accomplice of serious and flagrant violations of human rights or of cession of a part of the national territory.
High treason is punished by life imprisonment.

Article 321-2

Constitutes an attack on the Constitution any conduct violating the Constitution and its principles or any failure, in serious contravention of democratic and republican values.
An attack on the Constitution is punished by a term of imprisonment of twenty-one years to thirty years and a fine of one hundred million (100,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 321-3

The statute of limitations cannot be invoked by the person prosecuted when it is established that by virtue of the functions they have held, they were able to influence the course of prosecutions or prevent any prosecution against them.

CHAPITRE 2
