Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any person who publicly wears a costume presenting a resemblance of a nature to intentionally cause confusion in the mind of the public with military and paramilitary uniforms or those of any official exercising judicial police functions or auxiliary police forces.

Article 375-6

Is punished by a fine of fifty thousand two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any person who, without a public or authentic act, unduly attributes to himself an identity other than that resulting from the statements of his civil status documents.

Article 375-7

In the cases provided for in the preceding articles, the trial court may order the application of the provisions of article 214-17 of this law and furthermore that mention of the judgment be entered in the margin of authentic acts or civil status acts in which the title was unduly taken or the name altered.

Article 375-8

Is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any person who, exercising the profession of business agent or legal or tax advisor, includes or allows to appear his quality as a former magistrate, former judicial police officer, former notary, former bailiff, former lawyer, civil servant, former civil servant, military officer or former military officer on any documents or writings whatsoever used in the context of his activity.

Article 375-9

Are punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, the founders, directors or managers of a company or establishment with a commercial, industrial or financial purpose who include or allow to appear the name of a member of the government or of an institution with mention of his quality in any publicity made in the interest of the enterprise which they direct or which they propose to found.
Are punished by the same penalties, the founders, directors or managers of a company or establishment with a commercial, industrial or financial purpose who include or allow to appear the name of a former member of the government, a magistrate or former magistrate, a civil servant or former civil servant or a high dignitary with mention of his quality in any publicity made in the interest of the enterprise which they direct or which they propose to found.

CHAPITRE 6 : DES FRAUDES AUX EXAMENS ET CONCOURS PUBLICS

Article 376-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, any person who, by any means and in any form whatsoever, commits fraud in or on the occasion of an examination or public competition having as its object entry into a public administration or obtaining an official diploma.

Article 376-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, any person who through imprudence, negligence or non-observance of regulations facilitates fraud in an examination or competition.

Article 376-3

The imprisonment is six months to three years and the fine three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, when the fraud is committed on the occasion of an examination or competition other than public.

Article 376-4

The following are notably considered as fraud in an examination or competitive examination, all practices tending to: transmit, communicate, disseminate or sell examination papers, their answer keys or their solutions; substitute said examination papers, results or lists of candidates; modify by addition or removal of marks or names of candidates from lists relating to said examinations or competitive examinations; communicate a code or any sign to certain candidates, with a view to identifying them and attributing to them an undeserved mark;
corrupt a marker, an examiner, an invigilator, a chairperson of an examination board, a candidate or any person participating in the organisation of the competitive examination or examination; carry out any blackmail against an examiner, a marker, an invigilator, a chairperson of an examination board, a candidate or any person participating in the organisation of the competitive examination or examination; fraudulently use any means of communication.

Article 376-5

The attempt of the offences provided for in this chapter is punishable.

TITRE VIII

NARCOTICS AND DRUGS CHAPTER 1: HIGH-RISK DRUGS

Article 381-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven to twenty-one years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the cultivation, production, manufacture, extraction, preparation or transformation of high-risk drugs.

Article 381-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven to twenty-one years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs anyone who contravenes the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the import, export and international transport of high-risk drugs.

Article 381-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven to twenty-one years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the offer, putting on sale, distribution, brokerage,

sale, delivery on any basis whatsoever, sending, dispatch, transport, purchase and possession of high-risk drugs.

Article 381-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the use or possession of high-risk drugs for purposes of personal consumption.

Article 381-5

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five to ten years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs anyone who: facilitates for another person the illicit use of high-risk drugs, for consideration or free of charge, either by providing premises for this purpose, or by any other means. This applies notably to owners, managers, directors, operators in any capacity whatsoever of a hotel, furnished house, boarding house, drinking establishment, restaurant, club, circle, dance hall, any place of entertainment open to the public or used by the public or any other place, who tolerate the use of high-risk drugs in said establishments or their annexes or in said places. Fraudulent intent is presumed in the event of a positive check by a police service; issues prescriptions of complaisance for high-risk drugs; knowing the fictitious or complaisance nature of the prescriptions, dispenses high-risk drugs; by means of fictitious or complaisance prescriptions, obtains or attempts to obtain high-risk drugs.

Article 381-6

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who in any manner whatsoever and by any means, causes a person to consume high-risk drugs without their knowledge.

Article 381-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who transfers or offers high-risk drugs to a person for their personal consumption.

CHAPITRE 2 : DRUGS AT RISK

Article 382-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five to ten years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the legislative and regulatory provisions concerning the cultivation, production, transformation, import, export, offer, putting on sale, distribution, brokerage, sale, delivery on any basis whatsoever, sending, dispatch, transport, purchase and possession of drugs at risk.
In the event of offer or transfer to a person for their personal consumption, or in the event of use or possession for purposes of personal consumption, the imprisonment is two to five years and the fine five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 382-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who: facilitates for another person, the illicit use of drugs at risk, for consideration or free of charge either by providing premises for this purpose, or by any other means. This applies notably to owners, managers, directors, operators in any capacity whatsoever, of a hotel, furnished house, boarding house, club, circle, dance hall, any place of entertainment open to the public or used by the public or any other place, who tolerate the use of drugs at risk in said establishments or their annexes or in said places. Fraudulent intent is presumed in the event of a positive check by a police service;
issues prescriptions of complaisance for drugs at risk; knowing the fictitious or complaisance nature of the prescriptions, dispenses drugs at risk; by means of fictitious or complaisance prescriptions, obtains or attempts to obtain drugs at risk.

Article 382-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five to ten years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty-five million (25,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in any manner whatsoever and by any means causes a person to consume drugs at risk without their knowledge.

CHAPITRE 3 : PRECURSORS

Article 383-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five to ten years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who produces, manufactures, imports, exports, transports, offers, sells, distributes, delivers on any basis whatsoever, dispatches, purchases, sends or possesses precursors, equipment and materials, either with the aim of using them in or for the illicit cultivation, production or manufacture of drugs, or knowing that these precursors, equipment or materials must be used for such purposes.

CHAPTER 4: PROVISIONS COMMON TO ALL DRUGS

Article 384-1

Is punished by imprisonment of eleven to twenty-one years and a fine of fifty million (50,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs anyone who: facilitates by any fraudulent means the false justification of the origin of the resources or property of the perpetrator of the offenses provided for in this section;
knowingly provides assistance in any operation of placement, conversion or concealment of the proceeds or reconverts into the national economy the resources acquired through the commission of these offenses; acquires, holds or uses gains and resources, knowing that they come from one of the offenses listed in the preceding paragraphs.

Article 384-2

When serious indications suggest that a person is transporting drugs concealed in their body, judicial police officers and customs officials authorized to establish the offense may subject them to medical screening examinations.
Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, any person against whom there exist serious indications suggesting that they could be transporting drugs concealed in their body who refuses to submit to the medical screening examination.

Article 384-3

Anyone who by any means incites the commission of one of the offenses provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law, even if this incitement has not been followed by effects, is punished by the penalties provided for the offense.

Article 384-4

The attempt of one of the offenses provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law is punished as the completed offense.
The same applies to the agreement or association formed with a view to committing one of these offenses.

Article 384-5

Financial operations relating to one of the offenses provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law are punished as the offense itself.

Article 384-6

The penalties provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law may be pronounced even when the various acts constituting the elements of the offense were accomplished in different countries.

CHAPTER 5

PROVISIONS RELATING TO THE ABUSE OF NARCOTICS AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES

Article 385-1

The use without medical prescription of drugs under control is prohibited on the national territory. Any drug found in the possession of a person who uses it unlawfully is seized and confiscated by court decision, even if said person is not subject to prosecution.

Article 385-2

Notwithstanding the provisions of Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law, anyone who, unlawfully, purchases, holds or cultivates plants or substances classified as narcotics or psychotropic substances, the small quantity of which allows them to be considered as intended for their personal consumption is punished: if it is a plant or substance classified as a high-risk drug including cannabis oil, by a sentence of imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs; if it is a derivative of the cannabis plant other than cannabis oil, by a sentence of imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs; if it is a plant or substance classified as a risk drug, by a sentence of imprisonment of fifteen days to three months and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.
The person concerned may be exempted from penalty or from its execution: if they have not reached the age of criminal majority; if they are not in a state of recidivism.

Article 385-3

Any person who drives a land, river, or air motor vehicle under the influence of a drug, even in the absence of any external sign of this unlawfully consumed drug, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
Any person who refuses to submit to screening tests and verifications is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph.
When there is reason to apply the provisions relating to homicide and involuntary injuries, the penalties provided for in the first paragraph are doubled.

CHAPTER 6: PROVISION TO MINORS OF TOXIC CHEMICAL INHALANTS

Article 386-1

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, knowingly, provides to a minor one of the toxic chemical inhalants appearing on the list established by the minister responsible for health.

CHAPTER 7: PENALTY REGIME

Section 1: Causes of aggravation, exemption and mitigation of penalties

Article 387-1

The maximum penalties provided for in Articles 381-1 to 384-5 of this law are doubled when: the perpetrator of the offense belongs to an organized gang or a criminal association; the perpetrator of the offense participated in other illegal activities facilitated by the commission of the offense; the perpetrator of the offense used violence or weapons;
the perpetrator of the offense exercises a public function and the offense is committed in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of their functions; the offense is committed by a health professional or by a person responsible for combating drug abuse or illicit trafficking; the drug is delivered or offered, or its use is facilitated to a minor, or a mentally disabled person or a person undergoing detoxification treatment; a minor or a mentally disabled person participated in the offense; the drugs delivered cause death or seriously compromise the health of one or more persons; the offense is committed in a penitentiary establishment, a military establishment, an educational or training establishment, a hospital or care establishment, a social services center or in other places where schoolchildren or students engage in educational, sports or social activities, or in the immediate vicinity of these establishments and these places, as well as in places of worship; the perpetrator of the offense added to the drugs substances that aggravate the dangers; the perpetrator of the offense is in a state of recidivism.
Convictions pronounced abroad are taken into consideration for the establishment of recidivism.

Article 387-2

Any person guilty of participation in an association or an agreement with a view to committing one of the offenses provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law is exempted from penalties if, having revealed the existence of this association or agreement to the judicial authority, they thus make it possible to avoid the realization of the offense and/or the identification of the other persons involved.

Article 387-3

Apart from the cases provided for in the preceding article, any person who is the perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offenses listed in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this law who, before any prosecution allows or facilitates the identification of the other

guilty parties or after the commencement of prosecution allows or facilitates the arrest of the latter, shall be punished by half of the penalties provided for in said articles.
However, said person is also exempted from the fine and where applicable, from accessory measures and optional complementary penalties.

Section 2: Penalties and Accessory or Complementary Measures Article 387-4

In all cases provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this Act, the courts shall order the confiscation of seized plants and substances, which shall be destroyed or handed over to an authorized body for their lawful use.

Article 387-5

In all cases provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this Act, the courts shall order the confiscation of installations, materials, equipment and other movable property used or intended to be used for the commission of the offence, to whomever they belong, unless the owners establish that they were unaware of their fraudulent use.

Article 387-6

In all cases provided for in Articles 381-1 to 383-1 of this Act, the courts shall order the confiscation of the proceeds of the offence, movable or immovable property into which the proceeds are transformed or converted and, up to their value, lawfully acquired property with which the said proceeds are mixed, as well as income and other benefits derived from such proceeds, property into which they are transformed or invested or property with which they are mixed, to whomever such proceeds and property belong, unless the owners establish that they were unaware of their fraudulent origin.

Article 387-7

In the cases provided for in Articles 381-1 to 384-5 of this Act, the courts may impose: permanent prohibition from the territory or prohibition from staying in the territory for a period of ten years or more against any convicted foreigner;
prohibition from staying for a period of two to five years if the offence is a misdemeanour and five to twenty years if the offence is a felony;	prohibition from leaving the national territory for a period of six months to three years;	prohibition of civil, civic and family rights for a period of six months to three years;	prohibition from driving land, river and air motor vehicles and withdrawal of permits or licences for a period of six months to three years;	permanent prohibition or prohibition for a period of six months to three years from exercising the profession in connection with which the offence was committed;	confiscation of all or part of the lawfully acquired property of the convicted person, whatever its nature, movable or immovable, divided or undivided.
In the cases provided for in the first paragraph of Article 381-5 of this Act, the courts may order the confiscation of utensils, materials and furniture with which the premises were furnished or decorated.
In the cases provided for in Articles 381-1 to 382-3 and 384-3 of this Act, the courts may order the closure for a period of six months to three years of hotels, furnished houses, boarding houses, drinking establishments, restaurants, clubs, circles, dance halls, entertainment venues or their annexes, or any places open to the public or used by the public or any other place where these offences were committed by the operator or with his complicity.

Article 387-8

Without prejudice to provisions providing for more severe penalties, anyone who contravenes one of the prohibitions listed in Article 387-7 above or the closure of the establishment provided for in paragraph 3 of the same article shall be punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 387-9

When a drug addict is convicted of one of the offences provided for in Articles 381-1, 384-5, 385-1 to 385-3 of this Act, the court may, in replacement of or in addition to the sentence, order treatment or care measures appropriate to his condition. A regulatory text shall establish the procedures for implementing these measures.

Section 3: Special Provision Article 387-10

The classification of different drugs is determined by law.

BOOK IV: INTERNATIONAL CRIMES

TITLE I: WAR CRIMES

Article 411-1

For the purposes of this title, war crimes means offences committed against persons or property during an international or non-international armed conflict and in connection with that conflict, in violation of the laws and customs of war or international conventions applicable to armed conflicts.
These include in particular:	serious violations of the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949;	other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to international armed conflicts within the established framework of international law;	serious violations of Article 3 common to the four Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 in the case of an armed conflict not of an international character;	other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to armed conflicts not of an international character.

CHAPTER 1

WAR CRIMES COMMON TO INTERNATIONAL AND NON-INTERNATIONAL ARMED CONFLICTS

Article 411-2

Wilful attacks on life, the infliction of great suffering, attacks on physical integrity or health, torture or inhuman treatment including biological experiments, attacks on the dignity of the person including humiliating and degrading treatment, when committed against a person protected by the law of armed conflicts, shall be punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 411-3

Anyone who intentionally directs attacks against the civilian population as such or against civilians not taking direct part in hostilities and which result in death or serious injury to physical integrity or seriously endanger their health shall be punished by a term of life imprisonment.

Article 411-4

Rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence, committed against protected persons, shall be punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.
Where the acts committed have resulted in death or serious injury to physical integrity or health, the penalty shall be life imprisonment.

Article 411-5

Anyone who subjects persons of another party to the conflict who have fallen into his power to mutilations or to medical or scientific experiments of any kind which are neither justified by medical, dental or hospital treatment, nor carried out in the interest of those persons, and which cause death or seriously endanger their health, shall be punished by a term of life imprisonment.

Article 411-6

Anyone who conscripts or enlists minors into the armed forces or armed groups or uses them to participate actively in hostilities shall be punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs; this provision does not preclude the voluntary enlistment of minors over fifteen years of age in the national armed forces.

Article 411-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever intentionally directs attacks against: buildings, equipment, medical units and means of transport, and personnel using, in accordance with international law, the distinctive signs provided for by the Geneva Conventions; personnel, installations, equipment, units or vehicles employed in the context of a humanitarian aid or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, insofar as they are entitled to the protection that the international law of armed conflicts guarantees to civilians and civilian objects; buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals and places where the sick and wounded are gathered, provided that such buildings are not military objectives.
When these same offences have resulted in loss of human life or serious harm to physical integrity or health, the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 411-8

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment, whoever: kills or injures by treachery individuals belonging to the hostile nation or army; declares that no quarter will be given, threatens an adversary therewith or conducts hostilities on the basis of this decision.

Article 411-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever: pillages a town or locality, even when taken by assault; destroys or seizes the property of an adversary, unless such destruction or seizure is imperatively demanded by the necessities of the conflict.

Article 411-10

Are punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, the taking of hostages.
When these have resulted in death or serious harm to physical integrity or health, the penalty is life imprisonment.

CHAPITRE 2 : DES CRIMES DE GUERRE PROPRES AUX CONFLITS ARMES INTERNATIONAUX

Article 412-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever directs attacks against civilian objects, that is to say objects which are not military objectives as well as the destruction or appropriation of property not justified by military necessity and carried out on a large scale in an unlawful and arbitrary manner.

Article 412-2

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment whoever attacks or bombards, by whatever means, towns, villages, dwellings or buildings which are undefended and which are not military objectives.

Article 412-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, except in cases where the security of civilians or military imperatives so require, takes part either in the transfer, direct or indirect, by an occupying power of parts of its own civilian population into the territory it occupies, or in the deportation or transfer within or outside the occupied territory of all or part of the population of that territory.

Article 412-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever improperly uses a flag of truce, the flag or the military insignia and uniform of the enemy or of the United Nations, as well as the distinctive signs provided for by the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 and their additional protocols and, in so doing, causes loss of human life or serious harm to physical integrity or health.

Article 412-5

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment whoever: employs poison or poisoned weapons; employs asphyxiating, toxic or similar gases as well as all analogous liquids, materials or devices; uses bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body; employs weapons, projectiles, materials and methods of warfare of a nature to cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering or to strike indiscriminately in violation of the international law of armed conflicts.

Article 412-6

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs whoever uses the presence of a civilian or other protected person to render certain points, areas or military forces immune from military operations.

Article 412-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever deliberately starves civilians as a method of warfare, by depriving them of objects indispensable to their survival, including by intentionally impeding relief supplies as provided for under the Geneva Conventions.

Article 412-8

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever: declares abolished, suspended or inadmissible in a court of law the rights and actions of nationals of the adverse party; intentionally deprives a protected person of his or her right to a fair and regular trial.

Article 412-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever: compels a prisoner of war or a protected person to serve in the forces of the hostile power; compels the nationals of the adverse party to take part in the operations of war directed against their own country even if they were in the service of the belligerent power before the commencement of the war.

Article 412-10

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment, whoever intentionally launches an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause incidental loss of life among the civilian population, injury to civilians which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated.

Article 412-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever intentionally launches an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause incidental: damage to civilian objects which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated; widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated.

Article 412-12

Is punished by a term of life imprisonment, whoever kills or injures a combatant who, having laid down his arms or having no longer means of defence, has surrendered at discretion.

CHAPITRE 3 : DES CRIMES DE GUERRE PROPRES AUX CONFLITS ARMES NON INTERNATIONAUX

Article 413-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever passes sentences and carries out executions without previous judgment pronounced by a regularly constituted court, affording all the judicial guarantees which are recognized as indispensable.

Article 413-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who orders the displacement of the civilian population for reasons related to the conflict, except in cases where the security of civilians or military imperatives require it.

Article 413-3

The provisions of this chapter apply to offences committed in the context of an armed conflict, that is to say, a conflict which opposes either the armed forces of at least two States (international armed conflict), or in a prolonged manner on the national territory the governmental authorities and organized armed groups or, organized armed groups among themselves (non-international armed conflict). It does not therefore apply to other situations of internal troubles and tensions such as riots, isolated and sporadic acts of violence or acts of a similar nature.

TITLE II

OF THE CRIME OF GENOCIDE AND THE CRIME AGAINST HUMANITY CHAPTER 1: OF THE CRIME OF GENOCIDE

Article 421-1

Constitutes a genocide punished by a term of life imprisonment any one of the following acts committed with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnical, racial or religious group or a group determined on the basis of any other arbitrary criterion: murder; serious injury to physical or mental integrity; deliberately inflicting on the group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction in whole or in part; measures intended to prevent births; forcible transfer of children.

CHAPTER 2: OF THE CRIME AGAINST HUMANITY

Article 422-1

Constitutes a crime against humanity and punished by a term of life imprisonment, the fact for anyone to commit any one of the following acts in execution of a concerted plan as part of a widespread or systematic attack directed against any civilian population: murder; extermination; enslavement; deportation or forcible transfer of population; imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental provisions of international law; torture; rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity; persecution against any identifiable group or collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other grounds that are universally recognized as impermissible under international law; enforced disappearances; the crime of apartheid; other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering, or serious injury to physical integrity or to physical or mental health.

Article 422-2

The perpetrators of the offences defined in this title also incur the following penalties: confiscation of the proceeds, property and assets derived directly or indirectly from the crime, without prejudice to the rights of third parties in good faith; prohibition from the territory which may not exceed five years.

BOOK V: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST PERSONS

TITLE I: INTENTIONAL ATTACKS ON THE PHYSICAL INTEGRITY OF THE PERSON

CHAPTER 1: OF TRAFFICKING IN PERSONS AND ILLICIT TRAFFICKING IN MIGRANTS

Section 1: Of trafficking in persons and related practices Article 511-1

For the purposes of this section, trafficking in persons means the recruitment, transportation, harbouring or reception of persons, by means of the threat or use of force or other forms of coercion, by abduction, fraud, deception, abuse of authority or of a position of vulnerability, or by the giving or receiving of payments or benefits to achieve the consent of a person having control over another person, for the purpose of exploitation.
Exploitation includes, in particular, the exploitation of the prostitution of others or other forms of sexual exploitation, forced labour or services, slavery or practices similar to slavery, servitude or the removal of organs.

Article 511-2

Constitutes the offence of trafficking in persons: the recruitment, transportation, transfer, harbouring or reception of a minor for the purpose of exploitation, even if none of the means set out in Article 511-1 above is used.

Article 511-3

The consent of a victim of trafficking in persons as defined in Article 511-1 above does not eliminate the offence.

Article 511-4

Is guilty of trafficking in persons and punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits one of the acts provided for in Articles 511-1 and 511-2 above.

Article 511-5

The term of imprisonment is eleven years to twenty-one years and the fine two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, when the offence has been committed with one of the following aggravating circumstances: if the victim is a minor of at most fifteen years; if the person is particularly vulnerable due to a physical or mental deficiency, or their state of pregnancy, their advanced age or their state of health; if the act was committed by fraud or violence, by use of false capacity, false title or falsified or altered documents or false authorization; if the perpetrator uses narcotics or any other substance likely to alter the will of the victim; if the perpetrator is carrying a visible or concealed weapon; if the perpetrator is an ascendant or a person having authority over the victim; if the victim has been sequestered, deprived of food or exposed in a public or private place of recruitment; if the victim is exposed to dangerous, arduous work or the worst forms of child labour; if the perpetrator has committed sexual abuse on the victim.

Article 511-6

The penalty is life imprisonment when: the victim has died; the victim has suffered mutilation or permanent disability; the trafficking was for the purpose of organ removal.

Article 511-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months,

.

Article 511-8

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to one year, any beggar,
: using threats; simulating wounds or infirmities; ; entering a dwelling or its outbuildings without authorization of the owner or occupants; gathering, unless it be husband and wife, the infirm person and their guide.

Article 511-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs,

Article 511-10

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs,
hands over to individuals who incite or employ them to begging.
If it concerns the father and mother, the forfeiture of parental authority provided for by the provisions of the code of persons and the family may be pronounced.

Article 511-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs,

Article 511-12

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who induces a minor to leave the home of their parents, guardian or employer or encourages their delinquency.

Article 511-13

means any person who organizes or trains or diverts a person to deliver them to begging, exerts pressure on a person to beg or continue to do so, is accompanied by one or more young children with a view to deriving directly or indirectly a financial, material or any other advantage.

Article 511-14

Is guilty of exploitation of the begging of others and punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs any person who commits one of the following acts: organizes the begging of others with a view to deriving profit therefrom; derives profit from the begging of others, shares the benefits or receives subsidies from a person habitually engaged in begging; recruits, trains or diverts a person with a view to delivering them to begging, or to exert pressure on them to beg or continue to do so; recruits, trains or diverts, for purposes of personal enrichment, a person with a view to delivering them to the performance of a service in return for a donation on the public highway.
Is assimilated to the exploitation of the begging of others the fact of being unable to justify resources corresponding to one's lifestyle while exercising a de facto influence, permanent or not, over one or more persons engaged in begging or being in habitual contact with that person or those persons.

Article 511- 15

The maximum penalties provided for in Article 511-14 above are pronounced when the offense is committed against: a minor; a person particularly vulnerable by reason of a physical or mental deficiency, their state of pregnancy, their advanced age or their state of health; a person subjected to begging by constraint, violence or fraudulent maneuvers.
The attempt to exploit the begging of others is punishable.

Article 511-16

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any beggar referred to in Articles 511-7 and 511-8 above who is found in possession of weapons, instruments or objects suitable for committing crimes or offenses.

Article 511-17

There may in addition be pronounced against the perpetrators of the offenses of begging and exploitation of the begging of others, a prohibition on residence for a period which may not exceed five years.

Section 2: Illicit trafficking in migrants Article 511-18

Illicit trafficking in migrants means the act by any person or group of persons of organizing the transport by land, sea or air, the accommodation or transit of migrants without satisfying the legal conditions for crossing borders in order to derive directly or indirectly a financial, material or any other advantage and that the national territory serves as a zone of origin, transit or destination.
Illicit trafficking in migrants is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 511-19

Is punished by the same penalties, fraud or falsification, counterfeiting of visas, documents or travel documents or any other documents attesting to the status of resident or national of Burkina Faso or of a foreign country or granting the benefit of refugee, stateless person, displaced person or victim of trafficking in human beings status.

Article 511-20

The penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, when the offense is committed with a single one of the following circumstances: the act of endangering or risking endangering the life or safety of the migrants concerned; the act of subjecting the migrants to inhuman or degrading treatment, including their exploitation.

Article 511-21

The court seized of an offense referred to in this chapter, orders in addition, in the judgment or decision of conviction: the confiscation of the means of commission of the offense as well as the proceeds of the offense; the destruction of titles, travel documents and identification documents having facilitated the commission of the offense; the permanent withdrawal of license, approval or any other authorization or administrative document from any entity whatever its legal form or from any person whose activity has favored the commission of the offense.

Section 3: Protection of victims and witnesses Article 511-22

In the cases provided for in Article 511-21 above, the court seized may in addition pronounce the prohibition on residence and/or the temporary prohibition on exercising certain civic, civil or family rights.

Article 511-23

Any person having taken part in an association or an agreement with a view to committing one of the offenses referred to in this chapter is exempted from penalty if, having revealed the existence of this association or agreement to the judicial authority, they thus allow the identification of the other persons involved and/or to prevent the realization of the offense.

Article 511-24

For the protection of the identity and private life of victims and witnesses, the trial courts may order in camera proceedings.
The trial court may dispense victims or witnesses from appearing at the hearing or take useful measures for the protection of their identity and their private life.

Article 511-25

Victims of foreign nationality of the offenses referred to in this chapter may request their retention on the national territory on a temporary or permanent basis.

Article 511-26

Victims of the offenses referred to in this chapter, when they present particular vulnerability or are minors, are assisted before the courts of investigation and trial by a lawyer of their choice or appointed ex officio.

Article 511-27

For the exercise of the civil action, the public prosecutor may require the placement under guardianship or legal administration of minor victims whose legal representative is not known or does not present guarantees of safeguarding the rights and well-being of the child.

Article 511-28

There is instituted, by decree taken in the Council of Ministers, a national body for vigilance and surveillance in matters of combating trafficking in persons and related practices.

CHAPTER 2: SERIOUS VIOLATIONS OF LIFE OR PERSONAL INTEGRITY

Section 1: Torture and related practices

Paragraph 1: Punishment of torture and related practices Article 512-1

For the purposes of this section, the following definitions apply: State agent: one of the following persons, whether exercising their powers in Burkina Faso or abroad:
•	a civil servant or any other person entrusted with a public service mission;
•	a member of the security or defense forces;
•	any person vested with a public or elective mandate. practices related to torture: acts or omissions constituting cruel, inhuman or degrading punishment or treatment which are not acts of torture within the meaning of bullet point 3 of this article but which are committed by a State agent or any other person acting in an official capacity or at their instigation or with their express or tacit consent, including arbitrary arrest and detention. torture: any act or omission by which severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, is intentionally inflicted on a person for purposes such as obtaining from them or a third person information or confessions, punishing them for an act they or a third person has committed or is suspected of having committed, intimidating or coercing them or intimidating or coercing a third person, or for any reason based on discrimination of any kind, when such pain or suffering is inflicted by a State agent or any other person acting in an official capacity or at their instigation or with their express or tacit consent.

Article 512-2

Any person guilty of acts of torture or related practices is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to five years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs.

Article 512-3

Any person who commits torture or related practices in the following circumstances is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs: if the victim is under eighteen years of age; if the victim had a disability at the time of the acts; if the victim is a pregnant woman; if the victim is an elderly person; if it resulted in temporary disability.

Article 512-4

The perpetrator of torture or related practices with at least one of the following circumstances is punished by life imprisonment: if it resulted in the death of the victim; if the acts were committed during armed conflicts or internal disturbances; if the acts resulted in permanent disability.

Paragraph 2: Miscellaneous provisions Article 512-5

Burkinabè courts have jurisdiction to try and punish any person who commits an act of torture if: the act is committed on the territory of Burkina Faso; the act is committed on board a vessel registered under Burkinabè law or in respect of which a permit or identification number has been issued in accordance with such law; the act is committed on board an aircraft, either registered in Burkina Faso or leased without crew and operated by a person meeting the conditions to be the owner of an aircraft in Burkina Faso; the alleged perpetrator has Burkinabè nationality; the complainant or victim has Burkinabè nationality; the alleged perpetrator of the acts is in Burkina Faso after the commission of the acts.

Article 512-6

The competent authorities shall take measures to ensure the protection of the complainant and witnesses against any ill-treatment or intimidation.

Article 512-7

The victim is entitled to fair and adequate reparation and compensation, including the means necessary for their fullest possible rehabilitation. In the event of death of the victim resulting from an act of torture or related practices, the victim's beneficiaries are entitled to compensation.
Notwithstanding any criminal proceedings, the State has the obligation to provide reparation to victims.

Article 512-8

Any person who by action, inaction, refusal to act or any other means obstructs or attempts to obstruct the accomplishment of the missions assigned to the National Observatory for the Prevention of Torture and Other Related Practices, hereinafter referred to as the Observatory, established by Article 20 of Law No. 022-2014/AN on the prevention and punishment of torture and related practices, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 512-9

Any person who exerts or attempts to exert pressure, intimidation, threats, reprisals, violence on persons who have provided or are suspected of having provided true or false information or of having cooperated with the Observatory is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 512-10

Any person who, being informed of acts of torture or related practices in a place under their responsibility or within their jurisdiction, does not inform the Observatory is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Section 2

Intentional violations of life, intentional violations of physical integrity and harm to health

Paragraph 1: Intentional violations of life Article 512-11

Homicide committed intentionally is classified as murder.
Any murder committed with premeditation or ambush is classified as assassination.

Article 512-12

The murder of biological or adoptive father or mother or any other legitimate or biological ancestor is classified as parricide.

Article 512-13

Infanticide is the murder or assassination of a newborn child.
A child is considered newborn until the expiration of the period prescribed for birth registration.

Article 512-14

The act of attempting to take the life of a person by means of substances that can cause death, in whatever manner such substances have been used or administered and whatever the consequences, is classified as poisoning.

Article 512-15

The perpetrator of assassination, parricide, poisoning or infanticide is punished by life imprisonment.
Any person guilty of a murder committed for purposes of cannibalism, worship, occult practices or commerce is also punished by life imprisonment.
However, the mother, as principal perpetrator or accomplice in the assassination or murder of her newborn child, is punished in the first case by life imprisonment and in the second case by imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years, but this provision may not apply to her co-perpetrators or accomplices.

Article 512-16

Murder is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven to thirty years.

Article 512-17

Is punished by a sentence of life imprisonment any perpetrator of murder committed with one of the following aggravating circumstances: the victim is a minor under fifteen years of age; the murder is preceded or accompanied by rape, torture or acts of barbarity;
the murder is committed against a magistrate, an official of the national police, a military member of the gendarmerie, a member of the prison administration staff or any other person holding public authority, during the exercise of or by reason of their functions; the murder is preceded, accompanied or followed by another crime; the purpose of the murder is either to prepare or facilitate an offence, or to favour the flight or ensure the impunity of the perpetrator or accomplice of an offence.

Paragraphe 2

Intentional attacks on physical integrity Sub-paragraph 1: Intentional violence

Article 512-18

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from two months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, intentionally, inflicts injuries or strikes blows, or commits any other violence or assault, if such violence has resulted in an illness or total incapacity for personal work of more than seven days and less than twenty-one days.
If there has been premeditation or ambush, the imprisonment is the maximum of the penalty prescribed in the preceding paragraph.

Article 512-19

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs anyone who, intentionally, inflicts injuries or strikes blows, or commits any other violence or assault, if such violence has resulted in an illness or total incapacity for personal work of seven days or less and if there has been premeditation or ambush or when the acts were committed publicly.

Article 512-20

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from two years to seven years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, any perpetrator

of injuries or blows or other violence or assault that causes an illness or total incapacity for personal work of twenty-one days or more.

Article 512-21

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from two years to ten years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, any perpetrator of intentional blows and injuries and assault having resulted in mutilations, amputations or deprivation of the use of a limb, blindness, loss of an eye or other permanent infirmities.
If the blows struck or injuries inflicted intentionally have resulted in death without intention to cause it, the perpetrator is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from seven years to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 512-22

The penalty is life imprisonment, when the blows and injuries, violence and assault are carried out with premeditation or ambush and if they have resulted in mutilations, amputation or deprivation of the use of a limb, blindness or loss of an eye or other permanent infirmities.

Article 512-23

Anyone who intentionally inflicts injuries or strikes blows against an ascendant, their adoptive father or mother is punished: by a sentence of imprisonment from two months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs if the injuries or blows have caused no illness or incapacity or if they have resulted in a total incapacity for personal work of seven days or less; by a sentence of imprisonment from two years to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs if the blows and injuries have caused a total incapacity for personal work of more than seven days and less than twenty-one days;
by a sentence of imprisonment from five years to ten years, if the blows and injuries have caused a total incapacity for work of at least twenty-one days; by a sentence of imprisonment from ten years to twenty years, if the violence described above is followed by mutilations, amputations or deprivation of the use of a limb; by a sentence of life imprisonment if the blows struck or injuries inflicted intentionally without intention to cause death have nevertheless caused it.
In all cases of premeditation or ambush, the maximum penalty is pronounced.

Article 512-24

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who participates in a brawl, rebellion or seditious gathering during which violence is carried out having resulted in death without intention to cause it, unless they incur a more severe penalty as perpetrator of said violence.

Article 512-25

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from three months to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who participates in a brawl, rebellion or seditious gathering during which blows are struck or injuries inflicted, unless they incur a more severe penalty as perpetrator of said violence.

Article 512-26

In the cases provided for in articles 512-24 and 512-25 above, the leaders, perpetrators, instigators and provokers of the brawl, rebellion or seditious gathering are punished as if they had personally committed said violence.

Sub-paragraph 2: Other attacks on physical integrity Article 512-27:
Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from eleven years to thirty years, anyone who commits the crime of castration.
If death has resulted, the perpetrator is punished by a sentence of life imprisonment.

Article 512-28

The act of provoking or assisting the suicide of another is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from one year to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, when the provocation has been followed by suicide or an attempted suicide.
The sentence of imprisonment is from three years to five years and the fine from one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs when the victim of the offence defined in the preceding paragraph is a minor of fifteen years or less.
Natural or legal persons guilty of the offence provided for in this article also incur the additional penalty of prohibition from carrying out the activity of vocational training provider within the meaning of the labour code for a period not exceeding five years.
Propaganda or advertising, whatever the mode, in favour of products, objects or methods recommended as means of causing one's own death is punished by a sentence of imprisonment from one year to three years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
Sub-paragraph 3: Harm to health and related offences Article 512-29:
Is seropositive any person having a presence of HIV or anti-HIV antibodies in their body at the time of screening test.

Article 512-30

Is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, having knowledge of their serological status, refrains from informing their spouse or sexual partner.

Article 512-31

Any person who, knowing their HIV-positive status, refrains from informing their spouse or sexual partner and contaminates them is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
If death results from this contamination, the perpetrator incurs a term of imprisonment of eleven to thirty years.
If the contamination results from rape, the perpetrator incurs a term of imprisonment of eleven to thirty years.

Article 512-32

Any person who knowingly transmits or attempts to transmit HIV to any other person by any means whatsoever is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to ten years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs. If contamination of at least two persons results, the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 512-33

Any person who causes another person an illness or incapacity for personal work by administering to them, in any manner whatsoever, knowingly but without intent to cause death, substances harmful to health is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.
Where an illness or total incapacity for personal work of twenty-one days or more results, the penalty is imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
Where the substances administered cause either an apparently incurable illness, or the loss of use of an organ, or a permanent infirmity, the term of imprisonment is five years to ten years and the fine is five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

Where they cause death without intent to cause it, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.
In all the cases provided for in paragraphs 2, 3 and 4 above, the court seized may also order the prohibition from exercising family rights, the prohibition from residence and/or the professional prohibition for a period that may not exceed five years.

Article 512-34

Where the acts specified in Article 512-33 above are committed by an ascendant, descendant, spouse or successor of the victim or a person having authority over them or having custody of them, the terms of imprisonment are increased to:	two years to five years in the case provided for in paragraph 1;	five years to ten years in the case provided for in paragraph 2;	eleven years to twenty-one years in the case provided for in paragraph 3;	eleven years to thirty years in the case provided for in paragraph 4.
The court seized may also order the prohibition from exercising family rights for a period of between five years and ten years.

Paragraphe 3 : Des dispositions diverses Article 512-35

The attempt of the offences provided for in Articles 512-18 to 512-28 above is punishable.

Article 512-36

Murder, injuries and blows are excusable if they are provoked by serious blows or violence against persons.

Article 512-37

Murder, injuries and blows are excusable if they are committed in repelling during the day the scaling or breaking of enclosures, walls or entry of an inhabited house or apartment or their outbuildings.

Article 512-38

Murder committed or blows struck or injuries inflicted by one spouse on the other as well as on their accomplice at the moment when they are caught in the act of adultery at the conjugal home are excusable.

Article 512-39

The crime of castration is excusable if it was immediately provoked by an indecent assault committed with violence or by rape.

Article 512-40

Injuries and blows are excusable when they are committed on the person of an adult caught in the act of indecent assault carried out with or without violence on a child under thirteen years of age.

Article 512-41

The excuses provided for in Articles 512-36 to 512-40 above are mitigating excuses.

Article 512-42

Parricide is not excusable.

Article 512-43

In all the cases provided for in this section, the confiscation of weapons, objects and instruments used to commit the offence is ordered.

CHAPITRE 3 : DES ATTEINTES A L'EGARD DES FEMMES ET DES FILLES

Article 513-1

For the purposes of this chapter, the following are understood as:	violence against women and girls: any act of violence directed against persons of the female sex, and causing or likely to cause women and girls physical, sexual, psychological, moral, economic and cultural harm or suffering including the threat of such acts, whether in public life or in private life;	cultural violence: any harmful and degrading practice against women and girls drawing their justification from customs, traditions and religions;	economic violence: the act of using one's means to slow down or prevent the economic or financial development of any person or the act of preventing any person from enjoying their socio-economic rights;	moral and psychological violence: any behaviour, words and attitude that undermine the personality of the woman or girl, their image, self-esteem and inner balance;	patrimonial violence: any act or negligence affecting the survival of the victim and consisting in transforming, removing, destroying, retaining or misappropriating objects, documents, goods and values, patrimonial rights or economic resources intended to cover their needs and which may extend to damage caused to common or personal property of the victim;	physical violence: any act or behaviour that undermines the physical integrity of the woman or girl;	sexual violence: any sexual assault committed with violence, coercion, threat, on a woman or girl.

Section 1

Du rapt, des sévices, de l'esclavage sexuel, des violences morales à l'égard des femmes et des filles

Article 513-2

Abduction consists of a person forcibly removing a woman or girl with a view to imposing marriage or a union on her without her consent.
Any perpetrator of abduction is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.
Where the perpetrator of the abduction has engaged in sexual abuse or rape on the victim, the penalty is imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 513-3

Sexual abuse or torture consists of introducing a substance into the genital organs of a woman or girl, or applying an object or substance to the breasts of a woman or girl with a view to inflicting burns, lesions or suffering on her.
Any person who commits sexual abuse or torture is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 513-4

The offence of sexual slavery consists of compelling a girl or woman by coercion to submit to or engage in various sexual practices.
Any person who commits the offence of sexual slavery is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 513-5

Constitute moral and psychological violence against a girl or a woman: any statement or attitude, whether or not accompanied by physical aggression, the objective of which is to harm the self-esteem of the woman or girl, to denigrate her and reduce the victim to a state of helplessness or submission; gestures, words, writings, by which an indecent or malicious intention or a manifest will to cause material damage, to injure or to kill the woman or girl is signified; infringement of the rights to sexual health and reproductive health of the woman or young girl, limitation of the enjoyment of these rights, by means of coercion, blackmail, corruption or manipulation, in particular the prohibition of using contraceptive methods; repudiation or ill-treatment inflicted on a woman who gives birth to a child of a sex not desired by her spouse; ill-treatment inflicted on sterile women; prohibition without reasons based on the interest or stability of the household, from visiting her parents or receiving their visits; prohibition without valid reason from exercising a profession, practicing an income-generating activity, an associative and political activity; unequal treatment of wives in the context of a polygamous marriage.
Is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits one of the acts referred to in this article.

Article 513-6

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs anyone who chases away, sends away, rejects or inflicts ill-treatment on a girl or a woman accused or suspected of witchcraft.

Section 2: Female genital mutilation Article 513-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who harms or attempts to harm the integrity of the female genital organ by ablation, by excision, by infibulation, by desensitization or by any other means.
If death has resulted, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 513-8

The penalties are increased to the maximum if the perpetrator is from the medical or paramedical profession.
The court seized may furthermore pronounce against him the prohibition from exercising his profession for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 513-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who through his public speech, statements or writings, encourages female genital mutilation.

Section 3: Abortion Article 513-10

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, by food, beverages, medicines, maneuvers, violence or by any other means, procures or attempts to procure the abortion of a pregnant woman or woman presumed pregnant, whether she consented to it or not.
The term of imprisonment is five years to ten years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs if the perpetrator habitually engaged in such acts.
If death has resulted, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.
The penalty is life imprisonment if the perpetrator habitually engaged in such acts.
The court seized may furthermore pronounce professional prohibition and/or prohibition of residence for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 513-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the prohibition from exercising his profession pronounced pursuant to the last paragraph of the preceding article.

Article 513-12

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs, voluntary termination of pregnancy or the attempt, subject to the cases provided for in the following articles.

Article 513-13

Voluntary termination of pregnancy may at any gestational age be performed if a physician attests after examinations that maintaining the pregnancy endangers the health of the woman or that there is a strong probability that the unborn child will be affected by a disease or infirmity of particular severity recognized as incurable at the time of diagnosis.

Article 513-14

In case of rape or incest, if the materiality of the distress is established by the public prosecutor, the pregnant woman may request from a physician within the first fourteen weeks, the termination of her pregnancy.

Article 513-15

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, by any means of dissemination or advertising incites abortion.

Article 513-16

In case of conviction for one of the offenses provided for in this section, the court pronounces the confiscation of objects used for the abortion. It may furthermore pronounce the closure of establishment and/or the prohibition from exercising civil and family rights for a period which may not exceed five years.
Any conviction for attempt or complicity in the same offenses entails the same prohibitions.

Article 513-17

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who contravenes the prohibition with which he is subject pursuant to the preceding article.

Article 513-18

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, in the course of exercising his functions, has knowledge of one of the offenses referred to in this chapter, does not inform the competent judicial or administrative authorities.

Article 513-19

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, by threats of reprisals, obstructs a denunciation on the part of the persons referred to in Article 513-18 above.

CHAPTER 4: ACCUSATION OF WITCHCRAFT

Article 514-1

The accusation of practice of witchcraft is any imputation, to one or more persons, of facts of a magical, abstract, imaginary, supernatural or paranormal nature which cannot be materially or scientifically proven and which is likely to harm the honor, reputation, security or life of such persons.
The material element of the offense is characterized by any fact, any act qualified as charlatanism, occultism, by rites or statements, speeches and cries tending to accuse others of one or more facts of a supernatural or paranormal nature, which cannot be materially or scientifically proven.
The intentional element is deduced from the knowledge of the harmful consequences of the act of accusation on the victim such as dishonor, social exclusion, violence and assault.

Article 514-2

Are accomplices to the offense of accusation of witchcraft practice: those who have provided any means or instrument having served to allegedly detect a person as practicing witchcraft; those who have, with knowledge, aided or assisted the perpetrator or perpetrators of the action in the acts, who prepared, facilitated or consummated it; those who, having had knowledge of occult practices aimed at allegedly detecting persons practicing witchcraft, have not informed the administrative or judicial authorities; those who have harbored persons presumed to be perpetrators or co-perpetrators of the offense; those who have provided support for writings and statements accusing one or more persons of witchcraft practice.

Article 514-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, any person found guilty or accomplice of accusation of witchcraft practice.
The term of imprisonment is three to five years in cases where the accusation of witchcraft has given rise to: the social exclusion of the victim; blows, injuries and assault on the victim; damage to movable and immovable property.

In case of death of the victim, the offense is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million five hundred thousand (2,500,000) CFA francs.

TITRE II: OTHER OFFENSES AGAINST THE PERSON

CHAPITRE 1

THREATS, RISKS CAUSED TO OTHERS AND FAILURE TO PROVIDE ASSISTANCE

Article 521-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, by any means whatsoever, threatens conditionally with harm to persons constituting an offense that the law punishes with a criminal penalty.

Article 521-2

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, by any means whatsoever, threatens conditionally with harm to persons that the law punishes with a correctional penalty.
When the threat is made to a magistrate, an officer or agent of judicial police, a public or ministerial officer, an auxiliary of justice, in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions, the penalty is imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) francs to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
It is the same when the threat is made to a witness, to a victim or to any other person, either with a view to inducing them not to report the facts, not to file a complaint, not to give testimony, or by reason of the report, the complaint or the testimony.

Article 521-3

Are punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, conditional threats of harm to property that the law punishes with a criminal penalty.

Article 521-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two to ten years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, whoever threatens with death by anonymous or signed writing, image, symbol, emblem or any form of expression of death.

Article 521-5

For the purposes of this code, endangering the person of another is the act of deliberately exposing another to death or injury.
Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, the act of endangering the person of another.
The act of directly exposing another to an immediate risk of death or injury of a nature to cause mutilation or permanent disability by the deliberate violation of an obligation of prudence or safety imposed by law or regulation also constitutes endangering the person of another and is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph.

Article 521-6

Legal persons declared criminally responsible for the offenses defined in Article 521-5 above incur, in addition to the fine, the complementary sanctions applicable to them, notably the prohibition from exercising the activity that is at the origin of the commission of the offense.

Article 521-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, whoever without risk to himself or to third parties, can prevent by his personal action either an act qualified as a crime, or an offense against the bodily integrity of a person, voluntarily refrains from doing so.
Is punished by the same penalties whoever voluntarily refrains from providing to a person in danger the assistance that, without risk to himself or to third parties, he could provide either by his personal action, or by summoning help.

Article 521-8

The act of voluntarily obstructing the arrival of assistance intended to enable a person to escape imminent danger or to combat a disaster presenting a danger to the safety of persons is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million five hundred thousand (2,500,000) francs.

Article 521-9
