Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to two million five hundred thousand (2,500,000) CFA francs, whoever directly exposes another to a risk of death or injury, by the deliberate violation of an obligation expressly imposed on him by laws or regulations.

CHAPTER 2: MANSLAUGHTER AND INVOLUNTARY INJURIES

Article 522-1

The act of causing the death of another person through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or failure to observe a law or regulation constitutes involuntary manslaughter.
Anyone who commits involuntary manslaughter is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.
The penalty is imprisonment of one year to seven years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs when the offender acted while intoxicated, committed a hit-and-run offence or attempted by any other means to escape the liability he may incur.
With regard to vehicle drivers, the penalties are also one year to seven years of imprisonment and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs when:	the driver was in a state of intoxication or was under the influence of an alcoholic state characterized by a concentration of alcohol in the blood or in exhaled air equal to or exceeding the rates set by the legislative or regulatory provisions of the road code or other laws and regulations;	the involuntary manslaughter occurred because the driver attempted to escape or refused to submit to a road safety check;	it results from a blood analysis or verification that the driver had used substances or plants classified as narcotics;	the driver did not hold the driving licence required by law or regulation or his licence had been cancelled, invalidated, suspended or withheld;	the driver exceeded the maximum authorized speed;	the driver, knowing that he has just caused or occasioned an accident, did not stop and thus attempted to escape the criminal or civil liability he may incur.
The penalties are imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, when involuntary manslaughter has been committed with several of the circumstances mentioned in this article, concerning drivers.

Article 522-2

Anyone who causes to another person through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or failure to observe a law or regulation, injuries, blows, illnesses resulting in total incapacity for work of more than three months is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.
The penalties are six months to three years of imprisonment and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs for any vehicle driver when:	he was in a state of intoxication or was under the influence of an alcoholic state characterized by a concentration of alcohol in the blood or in exhaled air equal to or exceeding the rates set by the legislative or regulatory provisions of the road code or other laws and regulations;
the offence occurred because he attempted to escape or refused to submit to a road safety check;	It results from a blood analysis or verification that he had used substances or plants classified as narcotics.

Article 522-3

Anyone who causes to another person through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or failure to observe a law or regulation, injuries, blows, illnesses resulting in total incapacity for work of more than three months when the offence has been committed with one of the following circumstances is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs:	while intoxicated;	in case of hit-and-run offence;	by fraudulently attempting to escape the liability he could incur.

CHAPTER 3

VIOLATIONS OF INDIVIDUAL LIBERTY AND INVIOLABILITY OF THE HOME

Article 523-1

Anyone who, without order from the constituted authorities and outside the cases where the law permits or orders it, abducts, arrests, detains, sequesters a person or knowingly provides a place to detain or sequester a person is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.
If the detention or sequestration lasts more than one month, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 523-2

The maximum penalty provided for in paragraph 2 of Article 523-1 above is pronounced, if the arrest or abduction is carried out either while wearing a uniform or a regulatory or apparently regulatory badge, or under a false name, a false capacity or on a false order from the public authority.
The same penalty is applicable, if the arrest or abduction was carried out with the aid of a motorized means of transport or if the victim was threatened with death.

Article 523-3

The perpetrator is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, if the person abducted, arrested, detained or sequestered has been subjected to torture or bodily abuse.
If the torture resulted in death, the mutilation of an organ or any permanent infirmity, the perpetrator is punished by a term of life imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.
The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph are applicable if the person abducted, arrested, detained or sequestered disappears.

Article 523-4

Anyone who is guilty of enforced disappearance of a person is punished by a term of imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.
By enforced disappearance of a person is meant the arrest, detention, abduction or any other form of deprivation of liberty of a person or a group of persons, under conditions removing them from the protection of the law, by one or more agents of the State or by a person or a group of persons acting with the authorization, support or acquiescence of the State authorities, who then refuse to acknowledge that these persons are deprived of liberty or to reveal the fate reserved for them or the place where they are located.

Article 523-5

Anyone who, involved in the commission of an enforced disappearance, has effectively contributed to the recovery alive of the disappeared person or enabled the elucidation of cases of enforced disappearance or the identification of the perpetrators of an enforced disappearance benefits from a mitigating excuse.

Article 523-6

Anyone who, by fraud or with the aid of threats or violence against persons or property, enters or attempts to enter the home of another person is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.
The term of imprisonment is two years to ten years and the fine one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, if the violation of the home is committed either at night, or with the aid of climbing or breaking in, or by several persons, or with the carrying of a visible or concealed weapon by one or more of the perpetrators.

CHAPTER 4

VIOLATIONS OF HONOUR, CONSIDERATION OF PERSONS AND PRIVATE LIFE

Article 524-1

Any allegation or imputation of a fact that harms the honour or reputation of persons or of the body to which the fact is imputed, is defamation.
The following cannot give rise to prosecution for defamation:	parliamentary debates;	judicial debates;	pronouncements or writings produced before the courts;	the faithful and good faith reporting of these debates and speeches with the exception of defamation trials;
the publication of judicial decisions including those rendered in defamation matters;	the official report made in good faith by a person duly appointed to conduct an inquiry and within the framework of that inquiry;	the imputation made in good faith by a superior or his subordinate;	information given in good faith about a person or a third party who has a personal or official interest in knowing it or who has the power to remedy an alleged injustice;	criticism of a work, a performance, an opinion of any kind publicly expressed provided that such criticism does not constitute a personal attack.

Article 524-2

Any outrageous expression, term of contempt or invective that does not contain the imputation of any fact, is an insult.

Article 524-3

The intentionally false denunciation of a false fact likely to expose the person who is the subject of it to a sanction by the administrative authority or his employer or to judicial proceedings is slanderous.

Article 524-4

Defamation committed by speech, cries or threats uttered in public places or meetings, by writings, printed matter sold or distributed, offered for sale or displayed in public places or meetings by placards or posters displayed to public view, by any other means of dissemination is punished:	by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, if the defamation is committed against:
•	public administrations, constituted bodies, the armed forces, courts and tribunals;
•	one or more members of the government or constituted bodies, any	person	charged	with	a	public	service	or	mandate

temporary or permanent by reason of their functions or capacity and witnesses by reason of their testimony;	by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, if the defamation is committed by means of electronic communication;	by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs, if the defamation is committed against private individuals.
This article is applicable to defamation directed against the memory of a deceased person when the author of the defamation intended to harm the honour or reputation of the living heirs, spouse or universal legatees.

Article 524-5

Prosecution is initiated upon complaint by the victim or his legal representative.
Until final conviction, withdrawal of the complaint stops the exercise of the public prosecution.
When the truth of the defamatory fact is established, and that fact was committed by a public authority in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of its functions, the perpetrator of the offence is discharged from prosecution.

Article 524-6

Public insult committed against private individuals, by speech, cries or threats uttered in public places or meetings, by writings, printed matter sold or distributed, offered for sale or displayed in public places or meetings by placards or posters displayed to public view, by any other means of dissemination is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.
This article is applicable to insult made to the memory of a deceased person, when the author of the insult intended to harm the honour or reputation of the living heirs, spouse or universal legatees.

Article 524-7

Anyone who, by any means whatsoever, makes a slanderous denunciation against one or more individuals, to the judicial authorities, to administrative or judicial police officers, or to authorities having the power to follow up on it or to refer the matter to the competent authority, or to the hierarchical superiors or employers of the person denounced is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.
The court seized may, in addition, order the insertion of its decision in whole or in part in one or more newspapers at the expense of the convicted person.
If the fact denounced is liable to criminal or disciplinary sanction, prosecution for slanderous denunciation may be initiated by the victim either after judgment or decision of acquittal or discharge, or after order or decision of no case to answer, or after dismissal of the denunciation by the magistrate, official, superior authority or employer competent to give it the follow-up it was liable to receive.
The court seized is required to stay proceedings if prosecution concerning the fact denounced is pending.

Article 524-8

For the offences referred to in articles 524-1 to 524-7 above, the limitation period for public prosecution is three months from the commission of the acts or the last act of prosecution or investigation.
In the case referred to in paragraph 3 of article 524-7 above, the period runs from the date of the order or decision of no case to answer, the notice of dismissal without further action, the judgment or decision of acquittal or discharge.

Article 524-9

Anyone who, by any means whatsoever, has intentionally infringed the privacy of another person's private life is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs:	by capturing, listening to, recording or transmitting words spoken in a private place by a person without that person's consent;	by fixing, recording or transmitting without that person's consent, the image of a person in a private place.
When the acts set out above have been accomplished in the sight and knowledge of the interested parties without their objecting to it, when they were in a position to do so, their consent is presumed.
The same penalties apply to the act of keeping, bringing or allowing to be brought to the knowledge of the public or of a third party or using in any manner whatsoever any recording or document obtained by means of one of the above acts.
When the offence provided for in the preceding paragraph is committed through the press, the particular provisions of the laws and regulations governing these matters are applicable with regard to the determination of the persons responsible.
In all cases, the court seized may, without prejudice to compensation for the damage suffered, prescribe all measures, such as sequestration, seizure and others, appropriate to prevent or stop an infringement of the privacy of private life; these measures may, if there is urgency, be ordered in summary proceedings.

Article 524-10

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who engages in: the manufacture, importation, possession, display, offer, rental or sale of devices or technical equipment designed to commit the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 524-9 above;
the manufacture, importation, possession, display, offer, rental or sale of devices or technical equipment designed for the remote detection of conversations, enabling the commission of the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 524-9 above or intended for the illegal capture of data;	the manufacture, importation, possession, display, offer, rental or sale of devices or technical equipment designed to intercept, divert, use or disclose, intentionally, correspondence sent, transmitted or received by electronic means or to proceed with the installation of devices designed to carry out such interceptions.

Article 524-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who knowingly publishes, by any means whatsoever, a montage made with the words or image of a person, without that person's consent, if it is not evident that it is a montage or if it is not expressly mentioned.

Article 524-12

The attempt of the offences referred to in Articles 524-9 to 524-11 above is punishable.
Public prosecution may only be initiated upon complaint by the victim, their legal representative or their successors in title.
The court may order the confiscation of equipment used to commit the offence, any montage medium, any recording or document obtained using one of the methods provided for in Article 524-9 above.

Article 524-13

The provisions of this chapter do not apply to natural or legal persons falling under the status of the written press, online press and audiovisual press.

CHAPITRE 5 : DE LA VIOLATION DE SECRET

Article 525-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, the disclosure of information of a secret nature by a person who holds it either by status or by profession, or by reason of a function or temporary mission.

Article 525-2

The provisions of Article 525-1 above are not applicable:	in cases where the law requires or authorizes the disclosure of the secret;	to anyone who informs the judicial, medical or administrative authorities of deprivations or abuse, including when it involves sexual assault or mutilation, of which they have become aware and which have been inflicted on a minor or a person who is not able to protect themselves due to their age or physical or psychological incapacity;	to the doctor who, with the victim's consent, brings to the attention of the procureur du Faso the abuse or deprivations that they have observed, physically or psychologically, in the exercise of their profession and which allow them to presume that physical, sexual or psychological violence of any nature has been committed.
When the victim is a minor or a person who is not able to protect themselves due to their age or physical or psychological incapacity, their consent is not necessary;	to health or social work professionals who inform the competent authorities of the dangerous nature to themselves or to others of persons who consult them and whom they know possess a weapon or have expressed their intention to acquire one. Reporting to the competent authorities carried out under the conditions provided for in this article may not be subject to any disciplinary sanction.

Article 525-3

In the event that any person, knowing themselves to be infected with HIV, fails to voluntarily comply with the obligation to inform their spouse, the doctor must ensure that the disclosure is made. Failure to comply with this obligation by the doctor is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 525-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, anyone who, except in the cases provided for in Article 341-8, in bad faith, opens or destroys letters or correspondence addressed to third parties.
Is punished by the same penalties, anyone who, in bad faith:	delays or diverts correspondence that has arrived or not at its destination and addressed to third parties;	fraudulently takes knowledge of the content;	intercepts, diverts, uses or discloses correspondence sent, transmitted or received by electronic means or proceeds with the installation of devices designed to carry out such interceptions.
This article is not applicable to fathers, mothers, guardians or persons responsible with regard to non-emancipated minor children when they act in the interests of the latter.

TITRE III : DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA FAMILLE ET LES BONNES MŒURS

CHAPITRE 1 : DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA FAMILLE

Section 1 : Des infractions en matière de mariage Article 531-1

For the purposes of this code, marriage means any form of union between a man and a woman, celebrated by a civil registrar or celebrated according to customary or religious rules.

Article 531-2

Is qualified as moral, material or emotional abandonment, in violation of marital obligations and duties of relief and assistance, except by court decision, the act of deserting, for whatever reason, one's spouse.
Also guilty of material, moral or emotional abandonment is anyone who, for the reasons mentioned in the preceding paragraph, deserts:	a woman who is pregnant or menopausal, married according to customary or religious rules or who has had a continuous and stable relationship with her partner;	any person married according to customary or religious rules or who has had a continuous and stable relationship with their partner and whose particular vulnerability, due to their age, illness, infirmity, apparent physical or psychological deficiency or known to its perpetrator.

Article 531-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two to six months and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who is guilty of moral, material or emotional abandonment, in violation of marital obligations and duties of relief and assistance.

Article 531-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years, anyone who forces a person into marriage.
The penalty is imprisonment of one to three years if the victim is a minor. The maximum penalty is incurred if the victim is under thirteen years of age.
Anyone who contracts or facilitates a marriage under such conditions is considered an accomplice.

Article 531-5

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to nine hundred thousand (900,000) CFA francs, anyone who, being bound by a monogamous marriage, contracts another before the dissolution of that marriage.

Section 2: Domestic Violence

Article 531-8

Domestic violence is that exercised within the context of a relationship between spouses, cohabitants or between ascendants and descendants which is expressed through physical, psychological, sexual, economic and spiritual aggression such as: willful assault and battery; rape; deprivation of food or care; confiscation of civil status documents used for identification of the person; infringements of property rights; serious violations of provisions relating to family rights; infringements of freedom to work and freedom of belief.

Article 531-9

Prosecution for domestic violence is initiated upon complaint by the victim or their legal representative, upon denunciation made to the judicial authorities, or to any competent authority, or ex officio by the public prosecutor.
Anyone who commits an act qualified as domestic violence is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.
The penalty is imprisonment of two months to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, when such violence has resulted in an illness or total incapacity for personal work of more than seven days and less than twenty-one days.
When the victim is a minor, a pregnant woman, a disabled person, a legitimate or natural ascendant, or if the violence has resulted in an incapacity for personal work greater than or equal to twenty-one

days, the penalty is imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.
If the death of the victim or permanent disability has resulted, the penalty is life imprisonment.
The court seized may further order the prohibition from exercising civil and/or family rights for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 531-10

Any person who aids or assists the perpetrator of domestic violence is punished by the same penalties as the perpetrator.
Anyone who has knowledge of acts of domestic violence and who refrains from reporting them to the competent authorities is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Article 531-11

The act of harassing one's spouse, one's partner, one's cohabitant through repeated actions having the purpose or effect of degrading their living conditions resulting in an alteration of their physical or mental health is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to four years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs when these acts have caused a total incapacity for work of less than or equal to eight days or have not resulted in any incapacity for work.
The penalty is imprisonment of three years to five years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, when the acts have caused a total incapacity for work exceeding eight days.
The same penalties are incurred when the offense is committed by a former spouse, a former cohabitant of the victim, or a former female partner or former male partner.

Section 3: Family Abandonment

Article 531-12

Anyone who is guilty of moral, material or emotional abandonment, in violation of marital obligations and duties of relief and assistance, is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 531-13

The act by the father or mother of a family of abandoning, for more than three months, the family residence and evading, without legitimate reason, their legal obligations to the point of compromising the health, safety, morality or education of their minor child is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.
This period may only be interrupted by a return to the home implying the intention to permanently resume family life.
The following are punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs: the act, by an ascendant or any other person exercising parental authority or having authority over a minor of fifteen years, of depriving them of food or care to the point of compromising their health; the husband or cohabitant who, knowing his wife or cohabitant to be pregnant, voluntarily abandons her for more than three months without serious or legitimate reason.

Article 531-14

The following are punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs: the act, by a person, of not executing a judicial decision or a judicially approved agreement requiring them to pay for the benefit of a minor child, a descendant, an ascendant or the spouse a pension, a contribution, subsidies or benefits of any nature owed by reason of one of the family obligations provided for by law, by remaining more than two months without fully discharging this obligation; the act, by a person bound to the obligation to pay a pension, a contribution, subsidies or benefits of any nature, of not notifying their change of domicile to the creditor within a period of one month from such change.
In case of recidivism, the penalty of imprisonment is always imposed.

Article 531-15

In addition to the normally competent courts, the court of the residence of the abandoned person or beneficiary of the pension may hear prosecutions brought pursuant to the provisions of articles 531-13 and 531-14 above.
Prosecutions may only be brought upon complaint by the abandoned person or beneficiary of the pension or their legal representative with production of the title invoked. However, they are brought ex officio by the public prosecutor when the perpetrator of the offense is that legal representative.
Prosecutions are preceded by a formal notice to the debtor of the obligation or pension to comply within fifteen days.
This formal notice is carried out at the request of the public prosecutor by a judicial police officer in the form of an interpellation.
If the debtor is a fugitive or has no known domicile, this is mentioned by the judicial police officer and the matter proceeds.

Article 531-16

The father or mother who seriously compromises, through ill-treatment, through pernicious examples of drunkenness or notorious misconduct, through lack of care or through lack of necessary supervision, either the health, or the safety, or the morality of one or more of their children, is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, whether or not forfeiture of parental authority is pronounced against them.
For the offenses provided for in this section, the court seized may order the prohibition from exercising civic and family rights for a period which may not exceed five years.

CHAPTER 2: OFFENSES AGAINST CHILDREN AND VULNERABLE PERSONS

Section 1

Exposure or Abandonment of Children, Incapacitated Persons or Elderly Persons

Article 532-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to seven million (7,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who exposes or causes to be exposed, abandons or causes to be abandoned in a solitary place a child or an incapacitated person unable to protect himself or herself by reason of his or her physical or mental condition.
If the exposure or abandonment has resulted in an illness, infirmity or total incapacity of twenty-one days or more, the term of imprisonment is two to five years.
If it has resulted in a mutilation or permanent infirmity, the term of imprisonment is increased from five years to ten years.
If the exposure or abandonment has caused death, the term of imprisonment is increased from eleven years to twenty-one years.

Article 532-2

If the perpetrators are the ascendants or any other persons having authority over the child or incapacitated person or having custody thereof, the penalty is:	imprisonment of two years to five years in the case provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 532-1 above;	imprisonment of five years to ten years in the case provided for in paragraph 2 of Article 532-1 above;	imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-five years in the case provided for in paragraph 3 of Article 532-1 above;	life imprisonment in the case provided for in paragraph 4 of Article 532-1 above.

Article 532-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who exposes or causes to be exposed, abandons or causes to be abandoned in a non-solitary place a child or an incapacitated person unable to protect himself or herself by reason of his or her physical or mental condition.
If the exposure or abandonment has resulted in an illness or total incapacity of twenty-one days or more, the penalty is imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.
If the child or incapacitated person has remained mutilated or crippled or if he or she has remained afflicted with a permanent infirmity, the penalty is imprisonment of two years to five years.
If death has resulted therefrom, the penalty is imprisonment of five years to ten years.

Article 532-4

If the perpetrators are the ascendants or any other persons having authority over the child or incapacitated person or having custody thereof, the penalty is:	imprisonment of six months to two years in the case provided for in Article 532-3 paragraph 1 above;
imprisonment of one year to three years in the case provided for in Article 532-3 paragraph 2 above;	imprisonment of five years to ten years in the case provided for in Article 532-3 paragraph 3 above;	imprisonment of eleven years to twenty years in the case provided for in Article 532-3 paragraph 4 above.

Article 532-5

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, voluntarily, inflicts injuries, strikes blows or deprives of food or care a child under fifteen years of age to the point of compromising his or her health.
The term of imprisonment is two years to five years and the fine three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs, if the injuries, blows or deprivation of food or care results in an illness or total incapacity for work of twenty-one days or more, or if there has been premeditation or ambush.
If the perpetrators are the legitimate, natural or adoptive father and mother or other legitimate ascendants or any other persons having authority over the child or having custody thereof, the penalty is:	imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs when the illness or incapacity for personal work is less than twenty-one days;	imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs in case of illness or total incapacity for personal work of twenty-one days or more, premeditation or ambush.
If the violence or deprivation practiced has caused death without intention to cause it, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years.

If the violence or deprivation is practiced with the intention of causing death, the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 532-6

For the offenses referred to in this section, the court seized may pronounce the prohibition from exercising civil and/or family rights for a period that may not exceed five years.

Article 532-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, for a lucrative purpose or for any other advantage, provokes or incites the parents or one of them to abandon their child born or unborn or provides or attempts to provide his or her intermediation to have a child born or unborn taken in or adopted.

Article 532-8

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs, anyone who fraudulently benefits from social benefits and assistance intended for an elderly person.

Article 532-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who fraudulently misappropriates sums of money owed to widowers or widows and/or to orphaned children by way of a pension or the execution of a court decision.

Article 532-10

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits abandonment or exclusion from the family environment with regard to an elderly person.
If the perpetrator is a natural or legitimate descendant of the elderly person, the penalty is imprisonment of three years to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 532-11

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million five hundred thousand (2,500,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits abuse of the state of ignorance or the situation of weakness, either of a minor, or of a person whose particular vulnerability due to his or her age, an illness, an infirmity, a physical or mental deficiency or a state of pregnancy, is apparent or known to its perpetrator, or of a person in a state of psychological or physical subjection resulting from the exercise of serious or repeated pressure or techniques likely to alter his or her judgment, to lead this minor or this person to an act or an abstention that is seriously prejudicial to him or her.
When the offense is committed by the de facto or de jure leader of a group that pursues activities having the purpose or effect of creating, maintaining or exploiting the psychological or psychic subjection of persons who participate in these activities, the penalty is imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 532-12

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs, any person guilty of exclusion from the family environment with regard to a pregnant girl or who refuses a forced marriage.

Section 2: Offences tending to prevent the identification of a child

Article 532-13

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, knowingly, under conditions likely to make identification impossible, moves a child, conceals them, causes them to disappear or substitutes another child for them or physically presents them as born of a woman who has not given birth or of a man who, obviously, could not be the father.
When the perpetrator of the above-mentioned acts is a health care worker, the penalty is imprisonment of three years to ten years and a fine of three million

(3,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs. In addition, a prohibition from practising the profession may be imposed for a period not exceeding five years.
If it is established that the child did not live, the perpetrator is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

Section 3: Abduction and non-presentation of minors

Article 532-14

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who by violence, threats or fraud abducts or causes to be abducted a minor or leads them away, diverts them or moves them or causes them to be led away, diverted or moved from the places where they were placed by authority or to whose direction they were subject or entrusted.

Article 532-15

The penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs if the minor thus abducted or diverted is under thirteen years of age. However, if the minor is found alive before the conviction decision has been rendered, the term of imprisonment is reduced to five years to ten years.

Article 532-16

The penalty is life imprisonment, regardless of the age of the minor, if the perpetrator has himself paid or has had the purpose of having himself paid a ransom by the persons under whose authority or supervision the minor was placed.
The same penalty is applied if the purpose of the abduction was the execution of an order or a condition.
However, if the minor is found alive before the pronouncement of the conviction decision, the penalty is imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 532-17

In the cases provided for in Articles 532-14 to 532-16 above, the abduction is punished by life imprisonment if it was followed by the death of the minor.

Article 532-18

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, without violence, threat or fraud abducts or attempts to abduct or divert a minor.

Article 532-19

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, when a decision has been made on the custody of a minor by a court decision enforceable provisionally or definitively, or by a judicially approved agreement, the father, mother, or any other person who does not present this minor to those who have the right to claim them or who, even without fraud or violence, abducts them, diverts them or causes them to be abducted or diverted from the hands of those to whom their custody has been entrusted, or from the places where the latter have placed them. If the perpetrator had been declared deprived of parental authority, the imprisonment may be increased to three years.

Article 532-20

Any person who transfers their domicile to another place after divorce, legal separation or annulment of marriage when the children habitually reside with them, must notify any change of their domicile and any change of the children's residence to those who may exercise a right of visit or accommodation with respect to them by virtue of a judgment or a judicially approved agreement.
If they fail to give this notification within one month, they are punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Article 532-21

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, except in the case where the act constitutes a punishable act of complicity, anyone who has knowingly

concealed or removed from searches a minor who has been abducted or diverted or who has removed them from the authority to which they are legally subject.

Section 4: Circulation of minors Article 532-22

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any person who, managing an establishment prohibited to minors under eighteen years of age, allows them to enter.
In case of recidivism, the definitive or temporary closure of the establishment is pronounced. When the closure is pronounced on a temporary basis, its duration may not exceed five years.

Article 532-23

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, any person who, managing an establishment, serves alcoholic beverages to a minor under eighteen years of age even when accompanied by their parents or guardians.
In the latter case, the parents, guardians or representatives who accompany minor children are punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Article 532-24

When in a cinema or any other public place is shown a film prohibited in particular to minors under eighteen years of age or under thirteen years of age, a poster of fifty centimetres by twenty bearing exclusively the mention "Film prohibited to minors under eighteen years of age" or "Film prohibited to minors under thirteen years of age" must be posted in a very visible manner at the ticket counters above the price list or the schedule of showings or on the posters; mention must be made in a very legible manner in all advertising concerning the said film including trailers.
Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one month to three months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000)

CFA francs, any person who, managing a cinema, does not ensure the publicity of the prohibition under the prescribed conditions.
In case of recidivism, the temporary closure for a period not exceeding five years, or definitive closure of the cinema is pronounced.

CHAPTER 3: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST MORALS

Section 1: Offences against morals and sexual assaults

Article 533-1

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits a public outrage to decency.
Constitutes a public outrage to decency any sexual exhibition or any other intentional act contrary to good morals accomplished publicly or in a private place accessible to public view, likely to offend the decency and moral sentiment of persons who are involuntary witnesses thereof.
However, such an act committed in private, in the presence of a minor constitutes an offence of incitement of a minor to debauchery.
The attempt of public outrage to decency is punished by the same penalties.

Article 533-2

Constitutes an indecent assault any act of a sexual nature contrary to public morals committed directly and intentionally on a minor or with violence, constraint or surprise on an adult.

Article 533-3

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to seven years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, any indecent assault, consummated or attempted without violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor under thirteen years of age of either sex.
If the perpetrator is an ascendant of the minor or if he is one of those who have authority conferred upon him by his function or if he acted in a group, he is punished by a term

of imprisonment of seven years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-4

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, any indecent assault consummated or attempted without violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor from thirteen to fifteen years of age of either sex.
Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs any indecent assault consummated or attempted without violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor over fifteen years of age and under eighteen years of age of either sex.
If the perpetrator is an ascendant of the minor or if he is one of those who have authority over him or if he abused the authority conferred upon him by his function or if he acted in a group, he is punished by a term of imprisonment of four years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-5

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, any indecent assault consummated or attempted with violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor under thirteen years of age, of either sex.
If the perpetrator is an ascendant of the minor or if he is one of those who have authority over him or if he abused the authority conferred upon him by his function or if he acted in a group, he is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-6

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs any indecent assault consummated or attempted with violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor from thirteen to fifteen years of age, of either sex.

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs any indecent assault consummated or attempted with violence, constraint or surprise on the person of a minor over fifteen years of age and under eighteen years of age of either sex.
If the perpetrator is an ascendant of the minor or if he is one of those who have authority over the child, or if he abused the authority conferred upon him by his function or if he acted in a group, he is punished by imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-7

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, any indecent assault consummated or attempted with violence, constraint or surprise against persons of either sex.
If the perpetrator has authority over the victim or if he abused the authority conferred upon him by his function or if he acted in a group, he is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-8

The attempt of indecent assault is punishable.

Article 533-9

Sexual harassment is the act of imposing on a person, repeatedly, remarks or behavior with sexual connotations which either violates his or her dignity by reason of their degrading or humiliating character, or creates an intimidating, hostile or offensive situation against him or her.
Is assimilated to sexual harassment the act, even if not repeated, of using any form of serious pressure, with the real or apparent purpose of obtaining an act of a sexual nature, for oneself or for the benefit of a third party.
Sexual harassment is punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

The penalty is imprisonment of three years to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs when the acts are committed:	by a person who abuses the authority conferred upon him by his functions;	on a minor under eighteen years of age;	on a person whose particular vulnerability due to his age, an illness, an infirmity, a physical or mental deficiency or a state of pregnancy, is apparent or known to the perpetrator.

Article 533-10

Any act of sexual penetration, of whatever nature, committed on the person of another by violence, constraint, threat or surprise constitutes rape.
Rape is punished by a term of imprisonment of seven years to ten years and a fine of six hundred thousand (600,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-11

Rape is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs:	when it has resulted in mutilation or permanent disability;	when it is committed on a minor from thirteen to fifteen years of age at most;	when it is committed on a person whose particular vulnerability, due to his age, an illness, an infirmity, a physical or mental deficiency or a state of pregnancy, is apparent or known to the perpetrator;	when it is committed by an ascendant or by any other person having authority of law or fact over the victim;	when it is committed by a person who abuses the authority conferred upon him by his functions;	when it is committed by several persons acting as perpetrators or accomplices;
when it is committed with use or threat of a weapon;	when the victim was put in contact with the perpetrator of the acts through the use, for the dissemination of messages intended for an undetermined public, of an electronic communication network;	when it is committed in conjunction with one or more other rapes committed on other victims;	when it is committed by a person acting in a state of manifest intoxication or under the manifest influence of narcotic drugs.

Article 533-12

When rape is committed repeatedly on an intimate and habitual partner with whom the perpetrator maintains stable and continuous sexual relations or when said partner is in any physical incapacity to accomplish a sexual relation, the penalty is a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-13

Rape committed on a minor person under thirteen years of age of either sex, is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-14

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of nine hundred thousand (900,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, the act by teaching personnel or any educational system, of having a sexual relationship with a pupil, apprentice or trainee who is a minor of either sex.
If this sexual relationship results in the pregnancy of the pupil, apprentice or trainee, the term of imprisonment is seven years to ten years and the fine is three million (3,000,000) to six million (6,000,000) CFA francs.
The court seized may in addition pronounce a prohibition from exercising the profession of teacher or member of the educational system for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 533-15

Constitutes the offence of adultery, the act by a married person of having sexual relations with a person other than their spouse.
Any person convicted of adultery is punished by a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-16

Prosecution for adultery may only be initiated upon complaint by the spouse.
Withdrawal of the complaint terminates proceedings brought against the adulterous spouse and their accomplice.
Withdrawal occurring subsequent to a conviction that has become final stops the effects of that conviction both with regard to the adulterous spouse and their accomplice.

Article 533-17

Proof of adultery is established either by bailiff's report, or by official report of flagrante delicto drawn up by a judicial police officer, or by confession related in letters, correspondence or documents emanating from the accused or their accomplice or by judicial confession.

Article 533-18

Constitutes the offence of incest punished by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, the act of having sexual relations with one's ascendants or descendants without limitation of degree or with a brother or sister who is full, consanguineous or uterine.
Except in cases of notorious concubinage or incestuous marriage, prosecution may only be initiated upon complaint by a relative and only against the person or persons designated in the complaint.

Section 2: Of prostitution and corruption of youth

Article 533-19

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, whoever habitually incites to debauchery or favours the corruption of minors from thirteen to eighteen years of either sex or even occasionally of minors under eighteen years.

Article 533-20

Prostitution is the act by a person of either sex of habitually engaging in sexual acts with others for remuneration.
Is punished by a term of imprisonment of one month to three months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs, the act, by any means, including by an attitude even if passive, of publicly soliciting others with a view to inciting them to sexual relations in exchange for remuneration or a promise of remuneration.

Article 533-21

The attempt of the offences provided for in articles 531-1 to 533-20 above is punishable.

Article 533-22

Procuring is the act, by anyone, in any manner whatsoever: of aiding, assisting or protecting the prostitution of others; of profiting from the prostitution of others, sharing the proceeds thereof or receiving subsidies from a person habitually engaging in prostitution; of recruiting, enticing or diverting a person with a view to prostitution or of exerting pressure on them to prostitute themselves or continue to do so.
Procuring is punished by a term of imprisonment of three years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to six million (6,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-23

Is assimilated to procuring and punished by the same penalties as those provided for in article 533-22 above, the act, by anyone, in any manner whatsoever: of acting as intermediary between two persons one of whom engages in prostitution and the other exploits or remunerates the prostitution of others; of facilitating for a procurer the justification of fictitious resources; of being unable to justify resources corresponding to one's lifestyle while living with a person who habitually engages in prostitution or while being in habitual relations with one or more persons engaging in prostitution; of obstructing the action of prevention, control, assistance or re-education undertaken by qualified organisations with regard to persons in danger of prostitution or engaging in prostitution.

Article 533-24

Procuring is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs when it is committed: with regard to a person whose particular vulnerability, due to their age, an illness, an infirmity, a physical or mental deficiency or a state of pregnancy, is apparent or known to its perpetrator; with regard to several persons; with regard to a person who has been incited to engage in prostitution either outside the territory of Burkina Faso, or upon their arrival on Burkinabe territory; by a legitimate, natural or adoptive ascendant of the person who prostitutes themselves or by a person who has authority over them or abuses the authority conferred upon them by their functions;
by a person called upon to participate, by virtue of their functions, in the fight against prostitution, the protection of health or the maintenance of public order; by a person carrying a weapon; with the use of constraint, violence or fraudulent manoeuvres; by several persons acting in the capacity of perpetrators or accomplices, without their constituting an organised gang; through the use, for the dissemination of messages intended for an undetermined public, of an electronic communication network.

Article 533-25

The acts provided for in article 533-24 above are punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs when they are committed by an organised gang.
Procuring committed by resorting to torture or acts of barbarity is punished by life imprisonment.

Article 533-26

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs the act, by anyone, acting directly or through an intermediary: of owning, managing, operating, directing, causing to function, financing or contributing to financing an establishment of prostitution; of owning, managing, operating, directing, causing to function, financing or contributing to financing any establishment open to the public or used by the public, of accepting or habitually tolerating that one or more persons engage in prostitution inside the establishment or its annexes or seek clients there with a view to prostitution;
of selling or making available to one or more persons premises or locations not used by the public, knowing that they will engage in prostitution there; of selling, renting or making available, in any manner whatsoever, to one or more persons, vehicles of any nature knowing that they will engage in prostitution there.

Article 533-27

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who habitually receives one or more persons engaging in prostitution inside a hotel, boarding house, drinking establishment, club, circle, dance hall or place of entertainment or their annexes or any place open to the public or used by the public and of which he is the holder, manager or agent.
The same penalties are applicable to any person who assists said holders, managers or agents.
In all cases, the decision of conviction must order the withdrawal of the license of which the convicted person was a beneficiary. It may, in addition, order the temporary or permanent closure of the establishment.

Article 533-28

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, by gestures, words or by any other means, publicly solicits persons of either sex with a view to inciting them to debauchery.

Article 533-29

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who tolerates the habitual and clandestine practice of debauchery in premises or locations not used by the public which he has at his disposal in whatever capacity.

Article 533-30

For the offenses provided for in this section, the court seized may order the prohibition of the exercise of civil and/or family rights for a period which may not exceed five years.

Section 3

Sale of children, child prostitution and child pornography

Article 533-31

For the purposes of this section, the following are understood as:	sale of children: any act or transaction whereby a child is handed over by any person or group of persons to another person or to another group of persons in return for remuneration or any other advantage;	child prostitution: the use of a child for the purposes of sexual activities in return for remuneration or any other form of advantage;	child pornography: any representation, by whatever means, of a child engaging in explicit sexual activities, real or simulated, or any representation of the body of a child, for primarily sexual purposes;	pornographic material: any medium including methods and materials conveying or serving to convey a visual or sound representation or a description of a person engaged in a real or simulated sexual act, demonstration or performance;	victim of sale of children, child pornography and child prostitution: any child who has been the object of sale of children and/or child pornography and/or child prostitution;	witness to sale of children, child pornography and child prostitution: any person, eyewitness or indirect witness, that is to say having heard cries coming from the places where these offenses were committed.

Paragraphe 1 : De la vente d'enfants Article 533-32

A child means any human being under the age of eighteen years.
The age of the child is determined by the production of birth certificates, declaratory judgments or any other documents corroborated by medical expertise. In case of conflict as to the determination of age, the competent court seized shall assess sovereignly.
If the documents produced specify only the year of birth, it shall be considered as having occurred on the thirty-first of December of said year. If the month is specified, the birth shall be considered as having occurred on the last day of said month.

Article 533-33

Constitutes the offense of sale of children:	the act of offering, handing over or accepting a child, whatever the means used, for the purposes of exploiting him, transferring the child's organs for consideration, subjecting the child to forced labor;	the act of unduly obtaining, as an intermediary, consent to the adoption of a child, in return for remuneration or any other form of advantage.

Article 533-34

The sale of a child is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Paragraphe 2 : De la prostitution des enfants

Article 533-35

Constitutes the offense of child prostitution, the act of offering, accepting, procuring or obtaining a child for the purposes of prostitution.

Article 533-36

Child prostitution is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

Paragraphe 3 : De la pornographie enfantine

Article 533-37

Constitutes the offense of child pornography, the act by any person:	of offering, accepting, procuring or obtaining a child for the purposes of pornography as defined by law;	of producing, making, distributing, disseminating, importing, exporting, offering, ordering, selling, buying, storing, possessing or holding by means of a computer system or by any other medium child pornography;	of exposing pornographic material to a child to train him in sexual activities;	of using the Internet or any other computer technology to manufacture, disseminate, acquire, exchange images or pornographic materials depicting children;	of using the Internet or any other computer technology to lure a child online for the purposes of pornography;	of using the Internet or any other computer technology or material to view pornographic images depicting children;	of directing to pornographic material depicting children or of facilitating the possession or downloading of pornographic materials depicting children;	of refraining from taking measures to prevent the possession, viewing or downloading of pornographic materials depicting children;
of facilitating a child's access to images, documents, sound or a representation of a pornographic nature;	of habitually consulting or in return for payment for an online public communication service, making available images or videos of child pornography;	of making, for any adult person, sexual propositions to a child under fifteen years of age or to a person presenting himself as such by using a means of electronic communication.

Article 533-38

Child pornography is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.
In the case referred to in Article 533-37 dash 9, the term of imprisonment is eleven to fifteen years and the fine from two million five hundred thousand (2,500,000) francs to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

Paragraphe 4 : Des dispositions communes Article 533-39

Any person suspected either as perpetrator or as accomplice to one of the offenses referred to in this section, committed outside Burkina Faso, may be prosecuted and tried in accordance with Burkinabe law when the victim is of Burkinabe nationality.

Article 533-40

Any person who, having taken part in an association or agreement to commit one of the offences provided for in this section, may benefit from mitigating circumstances if, having disclosed the existence of this association or agreement to the judicial authority, they thereby enable the identification of the other persons involved and/or prevent the commission of the offence.

Article 533-41

Any person who, in the exercise of their profession or on any other occasion, has knowledge of cases of sale of children, child prostitution or child pornography, has the obligation to inform the competent judicial or administrative authority, under penalty of prosecution for complicity.

Article 533-42

In the event of a repeat offence of one of the offences provided for in this section, the penalty incurred is eleven years to twenty-one years' imprisonment and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to six million (6,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 533-43

The term of imprisonment is eleven years to twenty-one years and the fine three million (3,000,000) to six million (6,000,000) CFA francs, when the offence of sale of children, child prostitution or pornography depicting children has been committed in one of the following aggravating circumstances:	if the victim is particularly vulnerable due to a disability;	if it has resulted in temporary disability;	if the victim is sequestered, deprived of food or exposed in a public or private place;	if the act was committed by fraud or violence, by use of false capacity, false title or falsified or altered documents or false authorisations;	if the perpetrator uses narcotics or any other substance likely to alter the victim's will;	if the perpetrator has abused the authority conferred upon them by their functions or capacity in relation to the victim;	if the perpetrator is carrying a visible or concealed weapon;	if the perpetrator is an ascendant or a person having authority over the victim.

Article 533-44

The perpetrator of the offences referred to in articles 533-33, 533-35, and 533-37 incurs life imprisonment:	when the victim suffers mutilation or permanent disability;	when the victim's death results.

Article 533-45

The court seised shall order:	the confiscation of the means of committing the offence as well as the proceeds of the offence;	the confiscation of property or income derived from the offence.

Article 533-46

The court seised may in addition:	order a residence ban and/or temporary prohibition from exercising certain civic, civil or family rights;	order the temporary or permanent withdrawal of a licence, approval or any other authorisation or administrative document from any entity whatever its legal form or from any person whose activity facilitated the commission of the offence;	order the provisional or permanent closure of premises used for the commission of the said offences.

Paragraphe 5 : Protection of victims and witnesses

Article 533-47

The State guarantees the protection of victims and witnesses of sale of children, child prostitution and child pornography. No one may be subjected to attacks, disciplinary sanctions or dismissal for having testified to the acts defined in article 533-31.

Article 533-48

Victims are assisted either by a social worker, or by a recognised person working in the field of childhood and approved by the competent children's court, or by the person exercising parental authority. The child witness is assisted either by a social worker, or by a recognised person working in the field of childhood and approved by the competent children's court, or by the person exercising parental authority.

Article 533-49

For the protection of the identity and privacy of victims and witnesses, the trial courts may order proceedings in camera. The trial courts may dispense victims or witnesses from appearing at the hearing or take any useful measures to protect their identity and privacy.

Article 533-50

Victims of the offences covered by this section are assisted before the courts of investigation and trial by a lawyer of their choice or appointed ex officio.

Article 533-51

The public prosecutor may request that victims whose legal representative is not known or does not present sufficient guarantees for safeguarding the rights and well-being of the child be placed under guardianship, after, where appropriate, a social investigation conducted by the competent services.

Article 533-52

Children who are victims or witnesses of offences of sale of children, child prostitution or child pornography are not criminally liable.

BOOK VI: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST PROPERTY

TITLE I

THEFTS, EXTORTIONS, SERIOUS BANDITRY, FRAUD, BREACH OF TRUST, USURY, RECEIVING STOLEN GOODS, COUNTERFEITING

CHAPTER 1: THEFTS AND EXTORTIONS

Article 611-1

Any person who fraudulently misappropriates a thing belonging to another is guilty of theft.

Article 611-2

Theft is punishable by a term of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Article 611-3

Any building, dwelling, lodge, caravan, tent or cabin, even mobile, which, even if not currently inhabited, is intended for habitation and everything dependent thereon such as courtyards, farmyards, stables, buildings enclosed therein, whatever their use, is deemed to be an inhabited house.

Article 611-4

Any forcing, breaking, damage, demolition, removal of walls, roofs, floors, doors, windows, locks, padlocks or other utensils or instruments used to close or prevent passage and any kind of enclosure whatsoever is qualified as breaking and entering.
External breaking and entering is that by means of which one can enter houses, courtyards, farmyards, enclosures or outbuildings or private apartments or dwellings.
Internal breaking and entering is that which, after entry into the places mentioned in the preceding paragraph, is done to doors, internal enclosures, as well as to cupboards or other closed furniture.
Included in the class of internal breaking and entering is the simple removal of crates, boxes, bales in canvas and rope, and other closed furniture, which

contain any effects whatsoever, even though the breaking and entering was not done on the spot.

Article 611-5

Scaling is the act of entering any place, either over an element of enclosure, or through any opening not intended to serve as an entrance.

Article 611-6

All picks, imitated, counterfeited or altered keys or keys which were not intended by the owner or tenant for the locks of any kind to which the offender has used them are qualified as false keys.
The genuine key unduly retained by the perpetrator of the offence is also considered a false key.

Article 611-7

The perpetrator of a theft carrying a visible or concealed weapon is punishable by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.
The same penalty applies to the perpetrator who held a weapon in a motorised vehicle used to travel to the scene of the offence or to ensure their escape.

Article 611-8

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of three million (3,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits a theft with at least three of the following circumstances: if the theft was committed with violence or threats of violence or illegal wearing of uniform or usurpation of a position of authority; if the theft was committed at night; if the theft was committed jointly by two or more persons; if the theft was committed by means of scaling, external or internal breaking and entering, underground opening or false keys in a house, an apartment, a room or a dwelling inhabited or used for habitation or their outbuildings;
if the perpetrator of the theft secured the use of a motorized vehicle in order to facilitate his enterprise or favor his escape; if the perpetrator is a domestic servant even when he committed the theft against persons he was not serving, but who were either in his employer's house, or in the house where he accompanied him; if the perpetrator is an employee or apprentice in the house, workshop or store of his employer or if he is an individual working in the dwelling where he stole.

Article 611-9

Is punished by a term of imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who commits a theft on public roads or in vehicles used for the transport of passengers, mail or luggage or within the premises of stations, airports, landing or boarding docks when the theft was committed in the circumstances referred to in the preceding article.
If the victim suffered injuries the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 611-10

Are considered as public roads, the roads, tracks, paths or any other places dedicated to public use located outside built-up areas and where any individual may freely circulate at any hour of the day or night without legal opposition from anyone.

Article 611-11

Theft is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to ten years, and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs when it: is committed by several persons acting as perpetrators or accomplices, without constituting an organized gang; is committed by a person holding public authority or charged with a public service mission, in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions or his mission;
is committed by a person who improperly assumes the capacity of a person holding public authority or charged with a public service mission; is preceded, accompanied or followed by threats of violence or violence against others not having resulted in total incapacity to work; is committed in a place used or intended for the storage of funds, valuables, goods or materials; is committed in a vehicle assigned to public passenger transport or in a place intended for access to a means of public passenger transport; is preceded, accompanied or followed by an act of destruction, degradation or deterioration; is committed by reason of the membership or non-membership, true or supposed, of the victim in a particular ethnic group, nation, race or religion; is committed by a person voluntarily concealing all or part of his face in order not to be identified; is committed in educational or training establishments as well as, during the entries or exits of students or within a very close time thereof, in the vicinity of these establishments; is preceded, accompanied or followed by violence against others having resulted in total incapacity to work for eight days or less; is committed by means of scaling, external or internal breaking and entering, underground opening or false keys in a building even not used for habitation; is committed at night; is committed with illegal wearing of uniform or usurpation of a position of authority;
is committed during a fire or after an explosion, a collapse, a flood, a disaster, a revolt, a riot or any other disturbance; involved an object that ensured the safety of any means of public or private transport.

Article 611-12

Theft is punished by a term of imprisonment of five years to seven years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs when it is committed by an adult with the help of one or more minors aged thirteen to under eighteen years, acting as perpetrators or accomplices.
The penalties are increased to five years to ten years of imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs when the adult is assisted by one or more minors under thirteen years of age.

Article 611-13

Theft is punished by a term of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs when it involves: a movable object classified or registered pursuant to the provisions of the law relating to the protection of cultural heritage in Burkina Faso, or a private archive document classified pursuant to the provisions of the same law; an archaeological discovery made during excavations or fortuitously; a cultural property that belongs to the movable public domain or that is exhibited, preserved or deposited, even temporarily, either in a museum of Burkina Faso, a library, a media library or an archive service, or in a place belonging to a public person or a private person performing a mission of general interest, or in a building assigned to worship.
The term of imprisonment is twenty-one years to thirty years and the fine five million (5,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs when

the offense provided for in this article is committed with one of the circumstances provided for in Article 611-7.
The fines mentioned in this article may be increased up to half the value of the stolen property.

Article 611-14
